# Changelog

All notable changes to the agent-board-toolkit are documented here. The format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/), and the project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html) (pre-1.0: features minor-bump, fixes/refactors/docs patch-bump). See [`../VERSIONING.md`](../VERSIONING.md) for the full policy.

> **This changelog begins at [0.8.2].** Earlier releases (v0.4.x–v0.8.1) predate the changelog and are recorded only as git tags — run `git tag -l 'v*' --sort=-version:refname` and inspect each tag's release commit for their contents. They were not retro-documented here to avoid reconstructing summaries after the fact.

## [Unreleased]

- **card#6911** — **a tool's verdict no longer dies with the reader that stopped listening, and the class gets the gate that forbids the N+1th instance.** A bin writes its report to stdout and only afterwards reaches a verdict-carrying `exit`; a consumer that stops consuming — `| head -1`, a pager the operator quit — kills the writer at the write, and the caller gets a signal death in place of the verdict AND its explanation. **Worse than no guard, because the invocation looks like it worked.** **Fixed:** `bin/agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`, the one confirmed-and-unfixed instance, adopts the shape ratified for `gh-code-search` under card#6884 — `trap '' PIPE` as the first statement after `set`, plus one tolerated write per stream of an already-built string. Driven to a real STALE-COPIES verdict it now answers **rc 1 with 259 B of stderr** through `head -n 0`, where it answered **rc 141 with 0 B** before; on a passing tree the clean **rc 0** survives too, where truncation previously reported 141 — a runtime that is fine, reported as broken. **⚠ ONE OBSERVABLE CHANGE: its report is now BUFFERED** — nothing appears until the run ends, and it can spend up to 10 s per runtime in `git fetch`. Progressive output is what made the verdict losable; `--quiet`, the exit codes and the text are unchanged. **Added:** `tests/verdict-through-truncating-reader-selftest.sh`, which **measures** every shipped bin rather than grepping for the shape — the first sibling-audit instrument tried on this class failed its own control (it called the already-known-defective `gh-code-search` clean) and was discarded, and the card records that membership cannot be decided by reading. Each of the 21 files in `bin/` + `hooks/` is DRIVEN through a truncating reader and classified on rc PRESERVATION, with a stdout-byte **presence witness** that reds a driver which never reached a write (a fixture that cannot reach the condition it tests is how this class was misdiagnosed twice). **The remaining 11 known-defective bins are dispositioned by name, and the list is a RATCHET** — a member that starts surviving reds just as loudly as one that starts losing, so a fix cannot land without deleting its line and a new bin cannot join the class in silence. **Three prior wrong diagnoses of the mechanism are corrected on the record and planted as fixtures**: it is not builtin-vs-external (bash's `printf` builtin dies — measured); it is not `set -e` either (a bare builtin write with **no** errexit at all still dies, so `board-stats` is clean because it is *both* errexit-free *and* renders through children — naming only the errexit half excuses the commonest case); and **`rc 141` is not the mechanism and is never asserted on** — a GitHub Actions runner starts bash from Node with `SIGPIPE` set to `SIG_IGN`, a disposition that survives `execve`, so in CI the write returns EPIPE at rc 1 and any test expecting 141 passes locally and reds in CI. **That last one goes one level deeper than a hardcoded number, and the gate measures BOTH dispositions because of it:** the *outcome* differs too, not just the rc — a bin with no errexit is killed outright by the signal but only gets a non-zero `printf` it never inspects when the signal is ignored, so 11 of 14 driven members lose their verdict at `SIG_DFL` and only 8 do at `SIG_IGN`. Recording either alone would have produced a gate that reds in the other environment. Both legs are pinned with `env --default-signal` / `--ignore-signal` rather than `trap`, because **a signal ignored on entry to a non-interactive shell cannot be reset from inside it** — `trap - PIPE` is a silent no-op on a CI runner, so the first cut measured `SIG_IGN` twice while reporting two dispositions; the gate's own disagreement control caught it, and the gate now refuses (rc 1, naming the requirement) rather than run without the capability. `tests/runtime-check-selftest.sh` gains the verdict-path legs the general gate structurally cannot reach; `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` vendor surfaces unchanged.

- **card#6680** — **`kb_api` stops answering "the request did not complete" and "the server answered 500" with the same return code.** It returned **1** for a transport failure (curl exited non-zero — the request may never have arrived, and a write it DID deliver may already be committed) and **1** for any completed non-2xx (403/404/422/500 — the server answered, so the outcome is KNOWN and the status says why). One rc, two opposite epistemic states, and every caller in the tree buckets on a bare `|| …`. Measured through `kbcard`'s DL restamp pass: a 500 landed in the failed bucket and the run printed `kbcard: HTTP 500 on PATCH /tasks/9.json` and then, two lines later, `the request did not complete, so this run CANNOT say whether it landed` — the status the server answered with, followed by a denial that it answered. **`KB_HTTP` could not close this**: the caller shape this tree is built out of is `resp="$(kb_api …)" || …`, a command substitution whose subshell takes the global's assignment with it, which is why the signal has to ride the rc (asserted, so a future edit cannot move it back onto the global). **The rc now discriminates:** `0` a 2xx, **`1` THE SERVER ANSWERED non-2xx** (unchanged — this is what rc 1 meant on the path it was reached by most, so every `|| …`, `if !` and `if` bucket in every bin behaves byte for byte as before), and **`$KB_API_RC_TRANSPORT` THE REQUEST DID NOT COMPLETE**, with `KB_HTTP` at the `000` sentinel. Callers branch on the **name**, never the number; the number is pinned in the lib and is deliberately not `2` (every CLI here exits 2 for a usage error, so an rc propagated onward by a future `|| return $?` would read as one), not `3`–`5` (`fetch_board_cards`' separate rc vocabulary) and not curl's exit status, which is logged and never returned. **`kb_api_status` — the sibling — keeps `rc 0` and keeps its discriminator on the status line**, because `dl-a1-register-field` captures it with a bare assignment under `set -e` where any non-zero rc would kill the script before the status is read; `000` is now stated once as the sentinel BOTH functions use, and the transport arm sets `KB_HTTP` to it rather than leaving the previous request's status standing beside a status line saying `000`. **ONE call site changes behaviour: `board-stats`' changelog window** — the instance where the merged rc was already rendering a FABRICATED status. Its read was `resp="$(KB_API_QUIET=1 kb_api …)" || err="changelog read failed (HTTP ${KB_HTTP:-000}) …"`, and a command substitution is a subshell, so the `KB_HTTP` kb_api set inside it never reached that line: the status came from the lib's own empty initialiser and **every** failure rendered `HTTP 000` — the sentinel for a request that never completed — including the 403/500 the server ANSWERED, while `KB_API_QUIET=1` suppressed kb_api's own `HTTP 500 on GET …` line, so nothing anywhere in the run contradicted it. Measured before the fix, an answered 500 and an unopened connection produced the byte-identical `changelog read failed (HTTP 000) after 0 page(s)`, the 500 run with **empty stderr**. The page body now lands in a FILE rather than a `$(…)`, which is what lets `KB_HTTP` cross at all, and the arm branches on the rc: an answered non-2xx is `changelog read failed — the server ANSWERED HTTP 500`, a transport failure is `changelog read DID NOT COMPLETE … no answer was read, so this run cannot say why` — naming no status, because `000` is this state's own sentinel and not something the server sent. Every call here is a GET, so neither arm is a write-ambiguity. Asserted in `tests/board-stats-selftest.sh` as a DISTINCTION between the two messages in one run ("an error is reported" was already true of both, so it could not have failed on this defect), and watched RED under two mutants: the two messages merged back into the pre-fix line, and the body read put back through a `$(…)`. **`kbcard`'s restamp bucket is NOT split here** — that one changes what the pass reports to an operator about a WRITE, and is tracked on the card. **[vendor]** re-vendor `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh` **TOGETHER with `bin/board-stats`**: that bin now branches on `$KB_API_RC_TRANSPORT`, and under `set -u` a new `board-stats` beside an old lib dies on the unbound name at the first changelog read that fails. No other bin references the constant. **Two corrections in the tests this change touches, both to claims rather than to code.** `tests/fetch-board-cards-caller-claims-selftest.sh` cited `yes | head -c 50M | grep -q x` as the measurement behind *rc 141* for the SIGPIPE-eats-a-match shape it prescribes a herestring against — that command is **rc 1**: `yes` emits no `x`, so the grep never matches, never exits early, drains all 50MB and leaves its own `1` as the rightmost non-zero status, demonstrating nothing. The shape needs the reader to MATCH while the writer is still writing; `grep -q y` is the command that measures **141**, and the line now says so (the prescription itself was correct and is unchanged). `tests/kb-board-lib-selftest.sh` drops a companion assertion that `KB_HTTP` does not survive the caller's `$( … )`: a command substitution is a subshell by the language's definition, so no edit to this repo's code could red it — it passed against kb_api, against a kb_api that never touches the global, and against no kb_api at all. The half this repo DOES own, that kb_api sets the global to the `000` sentinel, is already asserted in the same block off a call that is not wrapped in `$( … )`, so restating it there would have been a second copy of one assertion rather than a second assertion.

- **card#6488** — **`release-artifacts-check` stops reading a real release PR as "version unchanged" when the version file carries a version-shaped line above its version line.** Classification is by version VALUE, and that value is the **first** `version_regex` match over a file **head owns** — so one prepended `# was 0.1.0` made the fork point and head extract the same value, the PR classified as `not a release PR`, and every declared member's leg silently did not run, at **rc 0**. Reproduced on the shipped script: an honest 0.1.0 → 0.2.0 release PR that forgets `NOTES.md` reds rc 1, and the same PR with one comment line above the version greens rc 0 — no config edit needed, which made it both a live defect on any repo whose version file grows a version-shaped comment and the cheapest evasion of the whole gate. The unsteerable half of the range is the **fork point's SET of matched values**, and a release must introduce a value there that head carries and the fork point does not, so on an equal classifying value the two sets are now compared — needing neither a content diff nor match positions: **exactly one value new at head NAMES the version being shipped** (a `::warning::` says so and every member is then asserted against it, so the gate *runs* rather than refusing), **more than one is rc 2** because which version is being shipped is then genuinely unknown, and **none is the ordinary unchanged verdict**. A value the fork point carries and head *drops* is a deletion, not a release, and deliberately does not enter the test — that is what keeps the predicate on "a value head introduced" rather than on "the version file changed at all", which would red every comment edit against a version nobody is shipping. **⚠ ONE CHANGE TO WHAT THE TOOL ACCEPTS OR REJECTS, fail-closed:** a PR whose version file gains a version-shaped value while its first match is unchanged was rc 0 and is now judged — rc 0/1 as a release of that value, or rc 2 where there are several. Nothing that classified as a release before classifies differently now. **The cost surface is the regex,** and it is priced in `docs/INSTALL.md` §6c: a `version_regex` matching more than the version declaration (a bare `[0-9.]+` rather than kanban's `'version' => '[0-9.]+'`) puts an ordinary PR in reach of the first rule, and the remedy — tighten it — must land in **its own PR**, since `version_regex` is read from the fork point. **Coverage:** the selftest's case 22 flips from pinning the defect (`rc 0`, `version unchanged (0.1.0)`) to pinning the fix, with the poisoned-but-otherwise-CORRECT release as the control that separates "classified as a release of 0.2.0" from "reds for some reason", three negatives that pin the direction (an echoed value, a dropped value, and one new value written twice) and the ambiguous rc-2 refusal. Five mutations of the new mechanism were each watched red and red exactly the checks they should: the test removed (pre-fix behaviour, 11), the direction flipped so dropped values count as new (12), the ambiguity classified instead of refused (2), the value list truncated to the first match (11), and the dedup removed (2). `release-artifacts/action.yml`'s `description:` CHANGED (its lockstep copy of the classification and fail-closed rules); `promote/action.yml` and every board-writing CLI are untouched.

- **card#6871** — **the `bin/*.py` entry points stop minting `__pycache__` inside the directory a consumer symlinks onto PATH.** `docs/INSTALL.md` §2 installs the toolkit by globbing `bin/*` and linking every entry onto PATH one by one, so **every entry of `bin/` is a PATH entry** and one that is not a tool is a defect the moment it exists. Three helpers were creating one on every run: `_kbc-may-archive.py`, `_kbc-archive-eligible.py` and `_kbc-stale-blocker.py` path-load `_kbc-archive-lib.py` with `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location`, and a by-path load **caches the compiled bytecode beside its target** — measured, one read-only `--help`-shaped invocation at rc 0 wrote `bin/__pycache__/_kbc-archive-lib.cpython-312.pyc` onto a clean tree. **A second directory was taking the same writes and is not even ours — by TWO routes needing two different fixes:** the shared lib path-loads the framework plugin's `kanban_common.py`, so a `__pycache__` appeared beside it under `~/.claude/plugins/…` (closed in-process by the same guard line); and `board-session-close` hands the plugin's **own** `kanban-reconcile.py` to `python3`, and that file `sys.path.insert`s its own directory and imports a sibling by name, caching `_stdio.cpython-312.pyc` into the plugin's `hooks/bin/` (reproduced directly). That file is foreign and cannot carry the guard line, but this repo owns the **invocation**, so that call now passes `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`. Downstream, the framework's install arm symlinked `bin/__pycache__` onto PATH and then refused the whole directory on the next run (that half is the framework's, roundtable #292 / its card#6865); on this side, `tests/lib-set-derivation-selftest.sh` had already had to grow a `grep -d skip` so its verdict would not depend on whether the maintainer had run a python helper. **`__pycache__/` was already gitignored, which is correct and is also what let this sit** — an ignored artifact is invisible to `git status` while still being globbed by every consumer of `bin/*`; nothing was ever tracked, so nothing is un-tracked here. **The fix is `sys.dont_write_bytecode = True` in each entry point's own module body**, and the rule is stated over ENTRY POINTS rather than over load sites because a module cannot suppress its own `.pyc` (the loader writes the cache while it *compiles*, ahead of running the module body) — so `_dependabot-reconcile.py` carries the line too though it path-loads nothing today, and `_kbc-archive-lib.py` does not, because there it could not work. These are one-shot CLI invocations, so the cache bought nothing: rc and output are asserted byte-identical either side of the change — for every entry point on its refusal path, and for one helper on a path that reaches the primitive and returns a decision, with the board-reading paths named as out of reach offline rather than implied. **New `tests/bin-artifact-hygiene-selftest.sh`** derives the whole `bin/*.py` population by glob on every run and — the part that decides whether it can actually go red — derives the **exemption** too, rather than pattern-matching it: a file is excused only if some other `bin/*.py` names it AND it carries no `__main__` marker at all, and that exemption's premise is then tested dynamically (a by-path load caches the `.pyc` regardless, so a guard line inside a library could not help). Anything else must carry the line, and a file the classifier recognises as neither is reported UNCLASSIFIED and **reds**. A first cut keyed on the single exact spelling `if __name__ == "__main__":`, which err-GREEN in the worst direction — a helper using single quotes, or with top-level code and no `__main__` at all, was filed as a library, excused, and dropped out of every dynamic leg as well. It also derives every `python3` invocation site in `bin/` + `hooks/` (joining backslash continuations, and resolving a `"$VAR"` target through its assignment) and requires each to resolve to a guarded sibling or to carry `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`, with an UNRESOLVED site reading red rather than counting as covered; each entry point is additionally run twice — as shipped and from a guard-stripped copy — with the mutant's write as the control that the clean run measured something, and a synthetic unguarded helper pins that the driver catches a load when there is one. `tests/_selftest-prelude.sh` exports `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` once for the suite, since several selftests path-load `bin/*.py` themselves; the new selftest clears it per probe, or its mutants could not write, and therefore asserts the **inherited** value at entry — nothing else in the suite could have seen that export go missing. **No `bin/` tool is added and no interface changes**, so no `PATH` symlink loop is owed.

- **card#6572** — **`install-board-hooks`' capability probe: the rule that a FAILED read is the measurement not happening now covers BOTH of its readers, and it is stated in ONE place rather than once per reader.** The probe reads the replaced entry twice — with the shell's `cat` and with git's own `hash-object` — and v0.28.0 established the rule for the native reader only: a read that fails is `git-read-failed`, INDETERMINATE, because scoring an absent measurement as a measured absence routes an operator into `--allow-copies`, a permanent pin to hook copies, over a local fault. **Reader 1 was never brought under it.** It read with `cat -- "$dst" 2>/dev/null || :`, and `|| :` throws away the only thing that distinguishes an entry the shell could not OPEN from one it read and found other content in — so an unreadable entry scored `shell does not track`, the pair read `yes:no`, and the verdict was **NOT_CAPABLE `readers-disagree-shell-does-not-track`**: a refusal naming a mechanism nobody measured, whose printed remedy is the copies opt-in. Measured on the unfixed installer, with a `cat` that fails on the probed entry: `--allow-copies` exits **3 with both hooks installed as copies**, i.e. the seat is pinned to hooks no `git pull` will ever reach, over a `cat` fault. **The fix is the rule, not the line.** Each reader is now `unmeasured` until its own read succeeds, and the one `case` over the reader PAIR — which already existed, and already defaulted to a refusal — is the single place a failed read becomes a verdict. A comparison is reachable only from inside a successful read, so no reader can score a measurement it never made; a third reader inherits the rule by inheriting the initialiser. Two new tokens join `git-read-failed`: **`shell-read-failed`**, and **`both-reads-failed`** for an entry neither reader can open (the ordinary shape of this fault — answering that one `git-read-failed` is true, incomplete, and earns the operator a second refusal on the re-run). All three are INDETERMINATE, rc **4** from the installer, and `--allow-copies` deliberately does not unblock them. **⚠ ACCEPTANCE CHANGE, two pair-rows.** Where the shell's read FAILS, the verdict moves from **NOT_CAPABLE rc 1 — with `--allow-copies` honoured at rc 3, installing copies** — to **INDETERMINATE rc 4, with the flag refused**: once where git also could not read (`unmeasured:unmeasured`), and once where git had already MEASURED `no` (`no:unmeasured`). The second reads as an over-refusal and is deliberate: `no:no` and `no:yes` both map to NOT_CAPABLE, so the silent reader could not have changed the verdict — but a verdict is not assembled out of a measurement that did not happen, and this is the mirror image of the precedent v0.28.0 already set, where a failed NATIVE read short-circuited to `git-read-failed` even when the shell had answered `no`. Both rows carry their own test. A seat in this state loses the copies escape hatch until the local fault is fixed. **What did NOT change is the other half of the same rule:** a read that SUCCEEDS and finds nothing is the seat answering, and stays a measured absence — `readers-disagree-shell-does-not-track`, NOT_CAPABLE — because the distinction is the read's *status*, not its output. That half is pinned by its own control (a `cat` that exits 0 having printed nothing), watched to red under a fix that treated every empty read as a fault. Both failure controls were watched to fail on the unfixed tool, and the both-readers case is forced by **one broken entry with neither reader stubbed** — a real symlink whose target does not exist. `docs/HOOKS.md`'s verdict table, the installer's own rc-4 remediation text and the Windows check's INDETERMINATE note all name a *reader* now rather than `git` alone.
- **card#6099** — **the two `pull_request` trigger properties this repo had been holding by hand become one gated contract: a trigger that observes fewer than ALL pull requests is REJECTED, and a workflow that reads a PR field must subscribe `edited` on every PR trigger it has.** Both were approved as class fixes for defects already fixed twice in place. **(a)** `tests/ci-matrix-parity-selftest.sh` admitted a workflow on `pull_request` regardless of `paths:` / `paths-ignore:` / `branches:` / `branches-ignore:`, so a filtered workflow's tests counted as "run on pull requests" for the PRs its own filter excludes — an err-GREEN gap that this file's header had ACCEPTED and documented rather than closed. **A deficient `types:` list narrows in exactly the same way and is now rejected on the same terms**: `types: [closed]` never runs on an OPEN pull request at all, and a list without `synchronize` never re-runs on a push, so the head commit that actually merges was never tested. (An earlier cut of this change treated `types:` as a separate question from coverage and *pinned that reading with a fixture* — the err-GREEN case asserted as correct, inside the guard written to prevent exactly that.) A narrowed trigger now qualifies for nothing: the workflow is reported with the key, or the missing types, that rejected it, and the tests it names read as unrun. A types SUPERSET (what both live gates carry) and a bare `pull_request:` (whose absent `types:` is GitHub's default set) stay admitted, pinned by fixtures, since the rule is otherwise indistinguishable from one that rejects everything. **(b)** A verdict that is a function of a PR field goes stale the moment the field is edited after opening, because the default event set never re-runs it: card#6054 shipped that defect on the BASE field and card#6062 on the TITLE. Any workflow reading `github.event.pull_request` must now subscribe `edited` on **every PR trigger it has — `pull_request` and `pull_request_target` alike**, since both fire on the same activity types and populate the same object; reading only the former reported a compliant `pull_request_target` workflow under a message no edit to it could ever clear. A reader subscribing NEITHER trigger is reported under its own true message rather than folded in. **The population is re-derived on every run from the parsed document's SCALARS** — never a list, and never the raw bytes. Walking scalars is what gets both boundaries right at once: a real YAML comment naming the field is not a read (pyyaml has already dropped it, which matters because this rule gets *described* in workflow comments — a raw-text match named `ci.yml`, whose own verdict reads no PR field, as a stale reader), while a `#` line inside a **block scalar** *is* one, because Actions expands `${{ }}` textually into the `run:` script before any shell sees it. A `#`-prefix line filter got the comment right and that one wrong — err-green, in this leg's own defect class. The population is exposed as its own projection and asserted non-empty with named members plus a member that must be OUTSIDE it, so an absence verdict cannot be measured over an empty set. **Both rules are VACUOUS on this tree, which is the whole risk:** no workflow is narrowed and both PR-field readers already carry `edited`, so on the live directory each answers `""` for structural reasons indistinguishable from a clean repo, and a check that cannot fail is a decoration. Each is therefore pointed at planted positives **watched to fail**: the filter keys and the required types come from the parser's own tuples through `filter-keys` and `required-types` projections (a hand-typed echo of a population is the shape filed at card#6645) and drive one fixture per member; twin pairs differing in the single token `edited` pin rule (b) on both PR triggers; and both defects are additionally planted **in a copy of the real workflow directory** (a `paths:` filter injected into `ci.yml`, `edited` stripped from `release-artifacts-gate.yml`) so the live assertions themselves are shown able to red rather than only their synthetic twins. Measured against **six mutants** of the parser — filter keys no longer narrowing (14 checks red), a deficient `types:` no longer narrowing (8), the population removed (12), the `edited` requirement removed (4), `pull_request_target` unread (3), the raw-text `#` filter restored (2) — with every negative staying green in each. **The bounds are stated in the file** rather than left to be inferred: the population of both rules is `.github/workflows/`, so the §6c recipe consumers paste is outside it; neither rule follows a local composite action a workflow `uses:` (verified at this change — `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` carry the literal in `description:` prose only, in zero `runs.steps`); and rule (a) admits `pull_request` only, so a `pull_request_target`-only workflow's tests read as unrun, which errs RED. `docs/INSTALL.md` §6c now states the `edited` obligation as the general rule it is, names `pull_request_target` beside it, and says where the toolkit's reach ends. Docs, CI and one selftest only: no `bin/` script, no vendored surface, no action input.
- **card#7113** — **`board-session-close` gains a fourth read-only leg: which OPEN card's own text still says it is blocked behind a card that has since become TERMINAL.** A card states a constraint — *blocked behind card#N* — that was **true when it was written**, nothing re-reads it when #N ships, and the next reader meets a stale hold and treats pullable work as blocked. Three instances were found by hand on this install (one card sat in the pull-me-next column reading as forbidden to pull); what was missing is anything that reads the citation. New `bin/_kbc-stale-blocker.py`, surfaced under a `── Stale blocker citations ──` section, is that reader, and it is **READ-ONLY** (every call is a GET; editing the stale card stays the agent's call) on the **archive leg's advisory contract**: a missing sibling warns, a non-zero exit warns, neither blocks the close — and, as the reconciler leg already states for itself, **a flagged card is not what a non-zero means**: findings ride the report at rc 0, so non-zero is the instrument (a board it could not read), never the boards. **The predicate is the whole design, and every part of it was measured against the live corpus (682 cards, 3 boards) before it was written, not chosen and then defended.** A citation is `card#N` / `card #N` / `cards #N`; **a bare `#N` is deliberately not one** — it is also a PR number, a roundtable issue number and a canon rule number here, and keying on it flags **27 of the 48 open cards** (every figure here carries its denominator on purpose — an earlier revision of this entry quoted `20`, which was over a narrower population that excluded the `waiting` lane while the tool's own definition of open is `lane_type != "done"`). A citation counts only in **blocking context**: one of twelve markers (`blocked`, `blocker`, `blocks`, `depends on`, `gated on`, `waiting on`, `queued behind`, `sequenced after`, …) on the same line, before it, with at most 60 characters of intervening text — the window is measured from the **marker's end to the citation's start**, so a longer marker does not buy a shorter reach — and no sentence boundary in between, because `card#N` **anywhere** flags those same 27 of 48, most `card#N` references in this corpus being **provenance** rather than blockers. **Bare `behind` and bare `after` are not markers** (*"Filed by the canon #7 sibling audit behind card#5410"* is provenance), and the markers match on **word boundaries**, which is load-bearing rather than tidy: without them `docblocks` contains `blocks` and a live card is flagged by its own prose about documentation — and they are matched **over the whole line, never over a truncated prefix of it**, because `\b` is evaluated against the string the regex is handed: scanning a 60-character slice made the cut position itself a word boundary, so `docblocks`/`roadblocks`/`unblocks`/`subblocker` became markers at exactly the one alignment where the cut fell mid-word, while the same slice shortened the window by the marker's own length so that a real `sequenced behind … card#N` reached only 44 characters and was silently dropped as *"no blocking marker"*. Both directions are fixture-pinned at the exact alignments that produce them (gaps 53 and 54, in both directions) and both are reproduced by one mutant. The window is a bound, not a fit — 40, 60, 80, 120 and **200** return the identical candidate set on the live corpus, **re-measured after that fix**, because the earlier sweep ran against the defective slice and could not have detected it; that is evidence the figure is not fitted to this corpus, not evidence the bound is idle. Three **structural** exclusions then apply, each independently justified and each independently reddable: a citation inside a **backtick span** (a quoted specimen is prose *about* the shape — this very card quotes the historical positive and would otherwise flag itself), inside a **`~~strikethrough~~`** (retracted text is text the author already withdrew), or on a line carrying **`UNBLOCKED` / `RESOLVED` / `DISCHARGED` / `✅`** (a line announcing the discharge is not asserting the constraint), whose **false negative is stated and deliberately not fixed** — `UNBLOCKED? no — still blocked behind card#N` is suppressed, as is any line using the ordinary word "resolved". A referent is **terminal** when its stage `lane_type == "done"` or it is archived/deleted, derived from each board's own stage shape — **no stage id is hardcoded**, and `waiting` (Shipped to dev) is **deliberately not terminal** — one of **three** stated under-reports, all printed in the report's own footer so the guard's stated scope equals its actual predicate: that one, the discharge-line exclusion above, and the inline-code exclusion, which drops a citation inside backticks **even inside a genuine blocking sentence** (``Blocked behind `card#6570` until it ships`` is ordinary style in this corpus, and separating a quoted specimen from a quoted reference is a judgement about the sentence rather than about the span). **⛔ Resolution is TENANT-SCOPED, and that is the load-bearing safety property, not a detail.** This kanban instance is **multi-tenant and card ids are allocated globally across every tenant's boards**, so a bare `GET /tasks/{id}.json` on a mis-cited id resolves to a **real card belonging to someone else** — measured live on this install (roundtable #327), where the only thing that stopped an automation acting on another tenant's card was a token-scope **403**, i.e. an accident rather than a guard. So the id→stage index is built **only** from the roster boards (in full, before any citation is resolved — citations cross our boards routinely, and a per-board scan answers "absent" for a referent the next board's read supplies), and the fallback single-card GET **validates `board_id` against the roster set**. **That set is DECLARED FROM THE CONFIG before any board is read, with the ids coerced to int** — a configured `board_id` is a *string* on this install while every card's is an *integer*, so an uncoerced roster is a set no card can belong to, and in a tenancy gate that reads as *"every referent is another tenant's"*. Seeding it instead from the boards that read **successfully** is the same sentence with a different meaning, and it is wrong: one unreachable board then turns every citation of its cards into an out-of-tenant finding, whose stated remediation is *"fix the citation"* — told to a reader whose citation was correct. Who a card **belongs to** is a fact about the config; whether this run can **decide** anything about it is a fact about the read; the two are tracked separately. So there are **five** outcomes, five distinct report states, never collapsed: a roster board that **was read** resolves normally; a roster board that **could not be read this run** is UNRESOLVED *in those words* — never out-of-tenant (it is ours), never a flag (nothing was decided); a **non-roster board is reported OUT-OF-TENANT**, naming both boards, and can never become a flag (an out-of-tenant citation is always a mis-citation — the fix is the citation, **not** a wider token); a **404** is UNRESOLVED; and **anything else** is UNRESOLVED with its state undetermined, because **a 403 is not a 404 and not an empty result** — the tri-state is the framework's own `fetch_card_status`, which gates on the HTTP status. A board body answering with an **id other than the one asked for**, or with no id at all, is refused rather than indexed under a key its stages do not belong to; a **duplicate `kanban.boards[]` entry** is declared once, so a config defect cannot double-count a board's cards, citations and findings; and a **non-integer** `board_id` is named and is a member of no population at all. The map is keyed by **`(board_id, stage_id)`** for the same reason: stage ids come from one server-wide sequence, so an unqualified key lets another board's stage answer for ours. The single-card reads are bounded (25/run, cached per id) and **a run that hits the bound says so**. **Every run prints its denominator** — boards read, cards indexed as referents, open cards scanned, citations examined, the disposition of every one of them, flags, out-of-tenant, unresolved, and GETs used — so a clean run states the population it was clean over; an empty result here is a measurement, not a silence. **Deliberately not built:** the `linked_tasks` relation-`blocks` leg the card proposed. Of the open cards, three carry `linked_tasks` at all and **every one is `relation_type: "caused"` — zero are `blocks`**, so that leg reports 0 forever at a cost of one detail GET **per open card**; and the `block_reason` field and the `Blocked/Gated` column are both empty across the corpus. A check that cannot fire is a decoration. **Also deliberately not built:** anything for the class's other half (a gate answered lower down in the same prose) — that has no machine-checkable predicate and is a writing rule; the docblock says so where the next reader will look. **Not wired into `board-snapshot`**, and that is stated as a non-goal in the file: that hook runs under a hard time budget at every session start. **Coverage:** new network-free `tests/kbc-stale-blocker-selftest.sh` — and unlike its archive sibling it **never SKIPs**, because this leg rides no framework primitive, so the fake `kanban_common` is wholly synthetic and every case runs on a bare runner. It pins the **positive control** (the known-true historical positive: `**Blocked behind DL-238 (card #6570)**` with #6570 in a done lane **must** flag), the negative control, the cross-board flag, the archived-referent flag through the detail read, the out-of-tenant finding, the bare-`#N` / provenance / word-boundary / sentence-boundary / window / done-card-not-a-citer controls, the `waiting` bound, the degraded-read (**not** 404) arm, the bounded referent read reporting its own bound, and the fail-loud board-read contract. **Nearly every one of those is an assertion of ABSENCE, which passes just as well against a tool that flags nothing at all** — so each is paired with a **mutant** run of the helper with exactly the guard it depends on removed, asserted to flag the case: the battery **counts itself** and prints the total on every run (`mutants run: N`), so no prose here carries a number that can go stale — it includes the one that reproduces the measured cross-tenant failure end to end (drop the tenancy gate **and** un-qualify the stage key, and another tenant's card at stage 3 borrows our board's lane type and is reported as a terminal blocker — confident, wrong, and about someone else's board). The mutation harness refuses a `sed` that changed nothing, so a mutant whose expression stops matching reds instead of silently proving nothing. **`tests/board-session-close-selftest.sh` gains a third delegation block** on the shape the archive and reconciler blocks already use, and both of those now plant a quiet fixture for the new sibling — without it their broad `sibling not found` absence needles would have been satisfied by the new leg's own warning, which is the same needle-discrimination trap the file already documents. **Canon #5, in the same change:** `_kbc-archive-eligible.py`'s two board-shape readers (`stage_field_maps`, `live_cards`) are **hoisted into `bin/_kbc-archive-lib.py`** at this second real caller rather than copied, because the `lane_type == "done"` classification IS the terminal-state predicate both legs decide on; the archive leg's behaviour is unchanged (its selftest passes untouched) and the lib keeps its name, since renaming a vendorable path with two existing path-loaders would cost every consumer a re-vendor to buy nothing. **Verified live, read-only, against the three real boards:** 684 cards indexed, 48 open scanned, 137 citations examined, **1 asserted as a blocker, 0 flagged, 0 out-of-tenant, 0 unresolved, 0 referent GETs** — the pre-committed acceptance shape. The one live candidate is a citation whose referent sits in Shipped to dev, i.e. exactly the `waiting` bound the docblock states, which is also the one **over-report shape the predicate cannot see** and says so out loud: marker→citation adjacency cannot tell *"blocked by card#N"* from *"this blocks card#N"*, where the citation is the blocked party. **⚠ VENDOR-BY-COPY CONSUMERS — one action, and it is the class this repo already shipped once as a v0.29.0 upgrade note:** **re-vendor `bin/_kbc-archive-lib.py` TOGETHER with any `bin/_kbc-*.py` helper you copied.** The board-shape readers `_kbc-archive-eligible.py` now calls live in that lib, so a **new helper beside an old lib** fails at runtime with `AttributeError: module '…' has no attribute 'stage_field_maps'` — and it fails at the call site, mid-report, not at startup. Which helpers path-load it is **derived, not listed** (`grep -l '_kbc-archive-lib.py' bin/*.py`), matching how [`docs/INSTALL.md`](INSTALL.md) §6b already derives the bash-lib set; that section gains the Python half in this change. **Consumer impact otherwise:** additive. `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` are untouched, no existing CLI's behaviour or exit codes change, and the new file is a `_`-prefixed private helper — so there is **no new `bin/` tool**, no README row, and **no `PATH` symlink loop owed**; a copy of `board-session-close` vendored without `_kbc-stale-blocker.py` still runs and says which leg did not run (advisory, never blocking).
- **card#6912** — **⚠ NARROWING, every lib-sourcing tool: `kb_is_uint` refuses a LEADING ZERO, so a value these tools used to accept is now refused.** The predicate was `^[0-9]+$` — a run of ASCII digits, any spelling — and every adopter hands what it accepts straight to bash arithmetic (`[[ x -gt 0 ]]`, `[[ a -eq b ]]`, `$(( ))`), where bash reads a leading-zero literal as **base 8**. Measured against the shipped lib: `kb_is_uint 010` said yes and the next `[[ 010 -eq 10 ]]` was then **FALSE** (`010` is 8), and `kb_is_uint 08` said yes and the next `[[ 08 -gt 0 ]]` **died mid-guard** with the raw `bash: [[: 08: value too great for base (error token is "08")` on the caller's stderr — a bash fault leaking out of the very guard written to prevent one. So the predicate was vouching for a string whose VALUE the next line then got wrong, or crashed on. **What a caller sees now:** `0`, `1`, `42`, `100` accept exactly as before — an interior zero was never the problem — while `00`, `08`, `007`, `010`, `0101` and every other zero-padded spelling get that tool's own ordinary refusal, in its own words and at its own documented rc, instead of a silently-octal reading or a raw arithmetic fault. That reaches every numeric ARGUMENT these tools take — a card/task ref, an external id, a swimlane id, a custom-field ref, a GitHub issue number, a throwaway sentinel id, a page size — each of which refuses a zero-padded value that one of them previously took; and a zero-padded number read **off the wire** (`meta.total`, `meta.last_page`, a `.data.id`) now falls to the same not-a-number arm those reads already have for a value they cannot read, instead of being compared as octal. The affected tools are the bins that source the shared lib — derive that set, never recall it; the lib header carries the one-line derivation. **Option (a), normalising inside the predicate, was considered and declined:** this is a PREDICATE, and its job is to answer truthfully about the shape it is handed, not to silently reinterpret the caller's spelling — a `010` that means ten and a `010` that is a typo are indistinguishable here, and only the caller knows which. Normalisation stays at the WRITE site (`$((10#$x))` the moment a value is accepted), where the tool that owns the input can also say what it did. Covered by a `kb_is_uint` block in `tests/kb-board-lib-selftest.sh`: the leading-zero cases and the `0`-still-accepts control were each watched RED under a mutation of the predicate before being watched green. **⛔ OPEN — one caller REGRESSES and it is not resolved here:** `gh-code-search --per-page 010` was accepted and normalised to ten under card#6884, with a measured justification tied to that tool's never-under-report commitment; it now refuses at rc 2 with `--per-page must be a whole number 1-100, got '010'`, the `$((10#…))` normalisation on the line after its shape guard is reduced to a provable no-op, the comment above that guard asserting the predicate "admits a leading zero" is now false, and 6 of that tool's selftest assertions are RED. Resolve before release.
- **card#6645** — **the "every value-taking flag" claim becomes an assertion: one derived, two-way gate replaces ten hand-typed flag lists.** Ten selftest blocks asserted that a bin refuses an empty value on *every* value-taking flag, over a list of flags typed into the test. A hand list cannot go red when the bin grows a flag, so each claim narrowed silently with every release — and two had already lost a member when this was written: `promote-stage-guard-selftest` named **five** of `promote-released-cards`' **six** guarded flags (`--cards`, shipped in v0.26.0, was never driven) under the sentence *"the whole class, not one instance"*, and `kbcard-selftest` drove **two** of `bin/kbcard`'s **27**. Ten repaired lists would have re-minted the defect at the eleventh, so the population is now DERIVED from the bin, once, in `tests/_selftest-prelude.sh`: `_value_flags <bin>` resolves every `require_value` / `kb_require_value` call site back to its flag — **both** spellings, because the three release movers are vendored standalone and carry their own copy of the guard — through the same-line `case` arm, alias arms split on `|`, and a backward walk to a multi-line arm or to the `if [[ "${1:-}" == "--flag" ]]` shape that `bin/kbcard`'s global `--board` uses; `expect_value_flags <bin> <flag>…` two-way-compares that against what each block names and reds in **both** directions, behind a positive control, because an empty derivation satisfies both comparisons while measuring nothing. An arm shape the predicate cannot resolve is emitted as an `UNRESOLVED:<line>` member rather than dropped, so it reds instead of shrinking the population in silence. **Adopted at ten call sites** (`promote-stage-guard`, `kbcard`, `release-pr-body`, `dl-a1-register-field`, `adopt-to-dl`, `release-artifacts`, `board-stats`, `dl-a0-backfill-triaged`, `next-dl`, `gh-code-search`), each proven to red on a planted `--bogus` arm in its bin and on a member removed from its list, then restored byte-identical and re-run green. Deriving the population rather than reading the lists also surfaced the one guarded flag in the toolkit that **nothing drove**: `kbcard`'s global pre-verb `--board`, which the bin's own header calls the highest-stakes instance of the class (an empty value falls through to the DEFAULT board, so `--board "$KEY"` with `KEY` unset is a wrong-board *write*, not a no-op). It is now driven as a real process in `tests/kb-positional-guard-selftest.sh` — the only file with a resolvable `kbcard` config — and watched to red with the guard deleted (without it the run reaches `curl`). New `tests/value-flag-derivation-selftest.sh` is the control on the derivation itself — a fixture per resolution rule, a negative control per non-rule (a guard call in a COMMENT, the guard's own definition, a flagless arm), the assertion driven to red in both directions, and one live-tree leg asserting that no `bin/` entry carries an unresolvable guard call. **Stated residual:** the predicate keys on the GUARD, so it answers *"is every guarded flag accounted for"*, never *"is every value-taking flag guarded"* — `bin/agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`'s `--reference` guards with `"${2:?…}"` instead and is invisible to it (a deliberate exclusion already reasoned in `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` § Stage C, not a new gap). The class is recorded there under *Post-program dispositions*. Tests and CI only; no `bin/` behaviour changes and no vendorable surface moves.

- **card#6645** — **`tests/hand-enumerated-population-census.sh`: a derivation that measures how many selftest blocks claim a total population while enumerating its members by hand.** A block that names its members literally cannot red when a new member appears, so the guard silently stops covering things and nothing fails. The instrument re-globs `tests/*.sh` every run and splits each file at its own banners — the METHOD ships, not a count, so a moving population cannot outrun it. A block counts iff it asserts TOTALITY (read from claim text only, never from executable code where the same words are data) AND enumerates LITERAL members. Carries a four-arm control: a planted totality+literal block must RAISE the count, a real candidate mutated to derive must LOWER it, and two further arms (totality with derived members; literal members with no totality claim) must move it NOT AT ALL — the pair that proves each conjunct discriminates alone rather than "any added block rises". Every sweep uses `/usr/bin/grep`, because a bare `grep` here wraps `ugrep --ignore-files`, silently honours `.gitignore` and exits 0 — a census scoping this class must not be truncated by the shape it measures. Diagnostic only: NOT wired into CI, and deliberately not named `*-selftest.sh` so the orphan gate does not claim it. The guards it indicts are separate work.

- **card#6884** — **`bin/gh-code-search`: a code search that refuses to report a count GitHub has told you it cannot stand behind.** `GET /search/code` answers **HTTP 200** with **`total_count: 0`** on a private repository, and it does so for a term that certainly EXISTS and for a term that cannot exist — byte-identically. Measured on two seats, two orgs, two tokens, with a public control returning a real count in the same window, and with `search/commits` answering on the same private repo with the same token (so it is not repo access and not the token). **The endpoint returns the same body for "the answer is none" and for "I could not answer",** the discriminator `incomplete_results` is already in that body, and the ordinary spelling — `gh api search/code --jq '.total_count'` — throws it away and hands back a `0` that reads as a finding. That is how a cross-repository claim ("this codebase never emits X") gets made from a search that never ran; it nearly landed as one. **Nothing in this repo called `search/code` before this change** (derived with a control: the same sweep DOES find `gh api` in `dependabot-deploy-reconcile`, `_dependabot-reconcile.py` and `board-session-close`, so the empty result was a measurement rather than a broken grep) — this is **preventive**, and what failed was an agent hand-rolling the query because there was no primitive to reach for. **The contract is `error / partial / complete` — never a count — and it is carried by the EXIT CODE, never by whether stdout looked empty:** **0** COMPLETE, **3** PARTIAL (`incomplete_results: true` with a NON-ZERO count **and at least one item** — the matched paths ARE printed and usable, the count is not authoritative, and the non-zero rc is what stops a caller consuming a flagged count it never said it would accept), **4** UNANSWERED (`incomplete_results: true` and **nothing to stand on** — a count of ZERO, an **EMPTY item set**, or both — fatal, and **no result set is emitted in any spelling**: no item line, no `items_returned=0`, and in `--format json` no `items` key at all, because an empty result set is precisely the wrong answer and a JSON consumer doing `.items | length` gets `0` from a `null` just as it does from `[]`. **Either half reading empty is enough:** the count is an ESTIMATE, so it is not evidence that results exist, and the item set is what was actually measured, so an empty one is not evidence that they do not — `{"total_count":17,"incomplete_results":true,"items":[]}` is rc 4 with `results=WITHHELD`, measured through the shipped bin), **1** ERROR, **2** usage. **A blanket fatal on `incomplete_results` was proposed and rejected**: collapsing "could not answer" into "answered partially" reproduces this tool's own subject one layer up and produces a guard people route around. **Three states, not two.** `gh` exiting non-zero (a 4xx, a secondary rate limit — which was roughly 2 of 9 probe attempts on the reference seat — no network, no auth) and a 200 whose body is not a readable code-search envelope are BOTH `ERROR`, never a zero: the envelope is type-gated on `total_count`, `incomplete_results`, `items` and each item's `path` + `repository.full_name`, so a rate-limit object, an HTML error page or a mistyped field refuses instead of arriving as `0`. **`total_count` is always tagged ESTIMATE and never appears without `incomplete_results` beside it** — same stdout line, same JSON object — so the two cannot be separated by a `cut`, a `--jq` or a copy-paste. Code search coarsens the figure with magnitude (`org:laravel use` → 12,864; `language:php array` → 80,740,352) and the estimate-ness is not confined to code search: `search/commits?q=laravel` returned 3,245,387 → 5,510,222 → 5,564,684 → 5,552,600 on one token in one session, non-monotonically. It is never an equality or threshold input; an exact figure needs `items` paginated. **⛔ No modulus and no stability heuristic are encoded, deliberately.** "Every `total_count` is divisible by 8" is refuted by a stable counterexample (`repo:laravel/framework class` → 2460, in a window that also returned 4472) and is asserted as BEHAVIOUR here — 2460 passes through byte-identically. "A `false` flag comes with a stable count" is confounded: on both seats every `false` sample was a consecutive repeat of an identical request, which ordinary response caching predicts exactly, so it belongs to a probe and not to a tool; the absence of a retry loop is what asserts it (exactly one request is issued, measured). **`incomplete_results` varies BETWEEN CALLS on one seat** — both values observed on one token for one query — so it is neither a seat nor an endpoint property and **its absence certifies nothing**. **Every refusal names the way through**, concretely when the query carries a `repo:` qualifier and with placeholders plus a stated reason when it does not: `repos/<owner>/<repo>/git/trees/<ref>?recursive=1` to enumerate paths, then `repos/<owner>/<repo>/contents/<path>` to read them — the route measured working (607 `.php` paths) on the same private repo and token that code search answers `0` for. **One request, no pagination, stated:** a page that comes back FULL is reported `items_truncated=true` and says so on stderr, because at that point the tool cannot know whether more exist and must not let a short set read as the whole set. `--per-page` 1-100 (default 100), `--format text|json`. Read-only; no git command, no board API, no writes. **Tests:** `tests/gh-code-search-selftest.sh` (**272** assertions, counted from a green run rather than carried forward — the figure this note first published, +170, was correct when written and was not re-derived when the file grew) drives the bin as a real PROCESS over a faked `gh` (`tests/_gh-code-search-stub.sh`, a tests/ file rather than a heredoc so `shellcheck` sees it) — **fixture-driven rather than live, and the reasoning is in the file's header**: one of the four states (`incomplete_results: true` with a NON-ZERO count) has never been observed live by either seat that measured this endpoint, the live positive is not stable between calls, the search API is rate-limited to a handful of requests a minute, and CI holds no token that can read an arbitrary private repo. An **opt-in live leg** (`GH_CODE_SEARCH_LIVE=1` plus a caller-named private and public repo — no repository is baked in) is the real-surface exercise, and it announces itself as SKIPPED rather than passing silently. **Thirty-seven mutations were applied, run and reverted byte-identically** (verified by `cmp`, not by `git checkout`, which would have deleted an untracked file). **Four of them started at ZERO red, and two of those were defects in the tool rather than gaps in the mutation set:** an `|| "$gh_rc" -ne 0` clause in the ERROR gate turned out to be unfalsifiable — the parse it guards runs only when `gh` exited 0 — and was REMOVED rather than tested (canon #6); the unknown-flag arm matched `--*`, so a single-dash typo (`-v`) became the QUERY and was sent to GitHub as a search term; and the "no item line leaks out of UNANSWERED" assertion could not fail against the real flagged-zero body, which carries no item, so a fixture pairing a flagged zero WITH an item exists to make it able to; and the fourth, from the second pass, is the inline `2>/dev/null || true` on the lib-missing refusal — no rc can distinguish it, because `set -e` is live inside the `{ }` following the final `||` and the failed write exits with the same 1 the refusal intends, so it is recorded in the test header instead of certified by a check that could not fail. The live leg was itself seen to fail on the real endpoint against a real private repo, and that run is what widened its predicate: with the flagged-zero branch mutated away the tool answered `PARTIAL total_count=0 … incomplete_results=true` at **rc 3**, which an rc-0-only assertion would have certified as clean. **Four defects an adversarial review returned before merge, each now watched to fail.** **(1) The guard lost its own signal to the consumer.** `gh-code-search … | head -1` — the shape this tool's own header invites — killed it mid-emit: measured **rc 141 with an EMPTY stderr** where a direct run gave rc 3 and 679 bytes of refusal, so both halves of the contract were destroyed by the READER, silently. `set -euo pipefail` plus a stdout write standing in front of the verdict-carrying `exit` was all it took, and a guard that looks like it worked is worse than no guard. SIGPIPE is now neutralised and each stream is written ONCE, from an already-built string, with a tolerated write; both properties are load-bearing and each reds on its own — removing only the tolerance converts the kill into a `set -e` death at **rc 1**, which is a VERDICT here and so is worse than the crash. **Sibling audit across `bin/`:** `board-snapshot` carries the same pipeline shape but exits 0, and `board-stats` measures CLEAN (no `set -e`, and it renders through child processes, so rc 1 and its stderr both survive a reader that stops) — but `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check` is **not**: driven to a real STALE-COPIES verdict it answers rc 1 with a `✗` line, and **rc 141 with zero stderr** through a reader that stops. Reported as its own class rather than fixed here. **(2) No query beginning with `-` was expressible at all.** The `-*` arm carried no `--` terminator, so `--` itself was refused as an unknown flag, and the comment justifying that arm asserted such a query could not exist. It can, and it answers: `q=-path:tests repo:laravel/framework class` returned `total_count: 1536` with `incomplete_results: false` on one live call. `--` is now the terminator, the disproved comment is DELETED rather than softened, and `--help` states the rule. **(3) Leading zeros reached base-8 arithmetic at three sites — ONE class, not three point fixes.** `kb_is_uint` tests the SHAPE and admits `010`; bash reads it as octal, so `--per-page 010` over a genuinely FULL ten-item page reported `items_truncated=false` at **rc 0 with no warning** — inverting the direction of error this tool commits to (it must err toward "there is more"). `--per-page 08` leaked a raw `[[: 08: value too great for base` to stderr, verbatim the failure its own guard was written to prevent. And a `total_count` of `0.0` or `1e2` satisfied the `type == "number"` gate, faulted the arithmetic test, and routed a FLAGGED ZERO into PARTIAL rc 3 **with its item line emitted** — this tool's own subject, arriving through its own gate. Fixed once, at the two WRITE sites: `--per-page` is normalised to decimal where it is accepted, and the envelope gate now requires `total_count`'s own RENDERING to be a run of digits (which covers jq's exponent form too, where `floor == .` would not). **(4) The readability gate accepted a multi-document body.** Two concatenated objects parsed as a two-line stream and only the first was read, so a flagged zero in the second was discarded behind a confident `COMPLETE … total_count=5` at rc 0. Closed with a slurp plus an explicit "exactly one document" clause. Not producible through a non-`--paginate` `gh api` call — a hole in the gate, closed as one rather than as a live defect. The withheld object's `reason` and `total_count_is_estimate` are now asserted too (both mutated to ZERO red before this, so the stated "every printed count is tagged ESTIMATE" invariant rested on nothing in the JSON format). **A SECOND adversarial pass then found that the first had closed the SIGPIPE class and left two un-audited direct writers standing behind it — in the file whose own rule says every byte goes through one of two tolerated writes.** **(a)** The `<query> is required` refusal wrote `echo "$USAGE" >&2` raw. With SIGPIPE neutralised an unwritable stderr makes that RETURN 1 rather than die, and as the last command of an `||` list it handed that status to `set -e` **before** the refusal could set its own: `gh-code-search $QUERY 2>&1 | head -1` with `QUERY` unset — the unexpanded-variable shape the diagnostic exists for — answered **rc 1**, which this tool's own table defines as the RETRYABLE error, for a command line that is wrong and will never clear on retry (measured; the sibling arm one line above, which routed through the tolerated writer, answered 2 throughout and is the control). **(b)** `trap '' PIPE` was set after the lib was sourced, so the lib-missing refusal ran in front of it: on a mis-vendored copy `--help 2>&1 | head -n 0` gave **rc 141 with an empty stderr** — verbatim the pre-fix failure, in the one state where that message is the entire product. The trap is now the first statement after `set -euo pipefail`, and the refusal carries its tolerance inline because it runs before the two writers exist. The header's hand-kept census of its own writers ("the only two writes in this file", while there were four) is replaced by a derivation a reader can run. **Also on that pass:** the UNANSWERED predicate stated above was widened to that union from `total_count == 0` alone. With the count as the sole discriminator, a flagged EMPTY item set reported `PARTIAL … items_returned=0` at rc 3 — printing the empty result set the withholding doctrine forbids, under a count that reads as an answer. A union rather than a swap: dropping the zero leg would let a flagged zero that happens to carry an item through, and that flagged zero is the live defect this tool was built for. Two boundaries are now STATED rather than implied: the tolerated write is untargeted, so a COMPLETE payload lost to a full filesystem on stdout exits **0** with no diagnostic — the verdict rides the rc and cannot be lost, the payload can — and "one verdict line in every state" excludes one reachable path, an unwritable `$TMPDIR` failing `mktemp`, which exits 1 with empty stdout. **The lib-sourcing-bins list is DELETED from all FOUR prose copies** (the lib header, `ADOPTION.md`, `docs/INSTALL.md` §6b, `docs/UPGRADE.md` §3) and replaced by the derivation each of them was a stale copy of — `grep -lE '^[[:space:]]*source "\$KB_LIB"' bin/*`, the same anchored pattern `agent-board-toolkit-drift-check`'s `MISSING-LIB` probe uses and `tests/drift-check-fixture-selftest.sh` asserts as a premise. **Four, not three: the first pass at this fixed three copies and declared the class closed against a denominator it had inherited rather than re-derived, and `docs/UPGRADE.md` §3 — the standing re-vendor recipe `INSTALL.md` §6b sends consumers to — survived it, naming six bins when the derived set was nine.** This is the exact drift `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` predicted: `gh-code-search` joined the set and reached ONE copy, so a consumer following any of the others vendored a bin set that omitted it and got the rc-1 "shared lib not found" refusal on every invocation of it. A hand-synced list is what a re-sync can only postpone, so the class now carries a gate rather than a fifth careful edit: **`tests/lib-set-derivation-selftest.sh`** runs every published spelling of that derivation against the tree and reds when one returns a different set (or nothing), and reds when a consumer-facing instruction line names members of the set instead of deriving them. It was seen to fail on both legs before it was believed — the release note you are reading spelled the pattern `'…"$KB_LIB"'`, where the unescaped `$` is an ERE anchor mid-pattern, so the published command matched **0** files where the correct spelling matches 9: an empty result reading as an answer, in the release note of the tool built to prevent exactly that. **A THIRD adversarial pass returned three findings that are ONE shape — a guard or a derivation whose STATED SCOPE is broader than its ACTUAL PREDICATE, which is this tool's own subject appearing in the guards written to prevent it.** Each is closed by making the claim and the predicate agree, never by leaving them apart. **(i) The writer census in the bin's header could not see the fd it claimed to cover.** It published `grep -nE '(echo|printf).*>&2|^_put'` while stating that its anchors were "exactly the boundary that matters: what reaches fd 1 or fd 2 directly" — but `>&2` is REQUIRED in the first alternative and `^_put` matches only the two names, so **no fd-1 write could ever appear in it**. Measured: a `printf` planted at top level between the flag parsing and the request produced **zero** census hits (five hits, four buckets, nothing left over) while collapsing every state to **rc 1** through `… | head -n 0` — COMPLETE 0→1, PARTIAL 3→1, UNANSWERED 4→1, verbatim the second pass's defect, returned through the one fd the rule could not look at. The selftest DID catch that mutation (70+ red), so the shipped tool was never unsafe; the rule a maintainer is told to apply was. The census is now `grep -nE '^[[:space:]]*(echo|printf)|(echo|printf).*>&2|^_put'` with a **fifth** bucket (an `echo`/`printf` inside a `$( { … } )` builder, now IN the census and disposed of by reading its enclosing construct rather than filtered out by the pattern — a **sixth** follows in (vii) below), the residual bound is stated where it is read — the `echo`/`printf` spellings plus anything redirecting to fd 2, and nothing about `cat`, `tee` or a mid-line `printf` — and a new selftest leg **extracts the command from the header** and runs it against a fixture carrying a planted top-level fd-1 writer, with the shipped pattern as the negative control, so a future narrowing reds instead of silently un-covering fd 1. **(ii) The gate that closes the hand-kept-list class carried a hand-kept list.** `tests/lib-set-derivation-selftest.sh`'s leg 3 was stated as *"**No** consumer-facing surface enumerates the set in prose again"* and ran over **four hard-coded paths**, so a fifth surface joined silently — measured, by appending a re-vendor sentence naming four bins to `README.md` and watching both `lib-set-derivation-selftest` and `readme-bin-coverage-selftest` stay at rc 0. Leg 3's population is now **derived on every run** (every readable non-binary file in the tree, `.git` excluded — leg 1's population) minus two carve-outs of different shape: version-specific frozen history, at that point carved **whole-file** (`docs/CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/UPGRADE.md` §6, `CLAUDE.md`'s release-snapshot table — a per-line disposition there would need a new entry every release, i.e. the hand-kept list again; that half measured wrong and is corrected in (v) below), and a small set of **per-line** dispositions, each keyed `<path>::<substring>` and each **asserted to still match a live line**, so a disposition cannot outlive what it disposed. Line scope is load-bearing on `README.md`, which carries one disposed paragraph and is the most consumer-facing file in the repo. Three mutations watched red, restored by copy + `cmp`: the reviewer's `README.md` line (1 red), a new `docs/` file nobody named (1 red), a disposition made stale (2 red). Leg 2 keeps its four-path list and it is stated as what it is — a FLOOR of surfaces that OWE the reader a derivation, an editorial obligation the tree cannot derive, which leg 3 no longer reads. **The class-closure ruling in `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` is corrected from "on the second attempt" to "on the THIRD"**, with the correction left visible: the lesson that pass recorded — *a class audit re-derives its denominator every pass* — had been discharged by a gate that fixed its own denominator at four. **(iii) The rc contract was stale in both consumer-facing statements.** The second pass widened UNANSWERED to the union and updated the bin's own exit-code table, but left the leading contract sentence at the OLD boundary in `README.md` and in this entry: both defined rc 4 as flagged **and** zero, so `{"total_count":17,"incomplete_results":true,"items":[]}` — **rc 4, `results=WITHHELD`, measured** — read as rc 3 with matches printed, and a caller writing an rc-3 branch from the README would mishandle the exact state the union was added to catch. That is the tool's INTERFACE, whose header says "GATE ON THESE". Both sentences now state the union, and this entry's later paragraph is reduced to the history of the change instead of restating the definition a second time (the contradiction between the two was itself the tell). **A FOURTH adversarial pass returned the SAME shape a fourth time — a stated scope wider than the predicate under it — now inside three of the guards the third pass had just shipped.** **(iv) The `docs/UPGRADE.md` split was cut on a section NUMBER while one sentence beside it called it "derived, not a line number" (three mentions of the split, one making the claim).** Only the OFFSET was derived; the **6** in `grep -nE '^## 6\.'` was a hand-kept fact — this gate's own subject, inside this gate. Measured: a new LIVE `## 6.` section carrying an enumeration line, with the history renumbered to `## 7.`, left the whole run at **rc 0 all-green** while the live region silently SHRANK, because the only premise beside the cut asserted what the live region **LACKS** — a direction that can catch a too-WIDE cut and never a too-narrow one. The cut is now located by the heading's **TEXT** with the number left free, and each split asserts how many headings matched (**exactly one** for `UPGRADE.md`, watched red at 0 and at 2) and that the matched heading lands in the **FROZEN complement**, which is the missing direction. **(v) The whole-file carve-out of `docs/CHANGELOG.md` covered its LIVE `[Unreleased]` section**, on a reason — *a frozen at-a-version list cannot rot* — that is simply false of the section the release in flight is written INTO: a regrown enumeration line under `[Unreleased]` passed at **rc 0** (measured, with the pre-fix gate re-run over the same plant as the control — also rc 0). Both files are now SPLIT the same way, by `_carve`, extracted at this second caller rather than copied: live head scanned, released tail out, and the identical line under a released heading stays green. What the split newly reports is **this entry itself** — one markdown paragraph, i.e. one line, naming most of `bin/` — and it is disposed **per-line** rather than by re-widening the carve-out, with the bound written beside it: the unit is the LINE, so the disposition covers the whole entry, and at release time the entry moves below the cut and the still-matches-a-live-line assertion turns it red, forcing the next author to dispose theirs deliberately instead of inheriting this ruling. **(vi) Leg 3's "fail-CLOSED by construction" was true of the FILE population and not of the per-file predicate.** That predicate is three literal strings (`_kb-board-lib`, `lib-sourcing`, `shared lib`), so a vendoring instruction spelled "the toolkit library file" or "the toolkit lib" is invisible to it — measured end-to-end with three probe files dropped into `docs/`, one sentence each, identical but for the noun: the `shared lib` one reported, the other two did not. **Stated, not widened** — every noun added drags in ordinary prose that names the same things without instructing anyone to copy anything, and a check that reports its own explanation gets suppressed rather than obeyed — so the bound now sits beside the predicate and the three sentences carrying the bound were narrowed to the FILE population, which is what is total. ⛔ THREE copies of the repo-scope claim were NOT narrowed and are knowingly left: leg 3's banner, its `THE POPULATION IS THE WHOLE REPO` paragraph and its top-level assertion label — the copies a reader actually meets. Single-sourcing them is card#6645 (sub-class A, machine-checkable), not a hand re-sync here. **(vii) The writer census disposed 23 of its 31 hits.** The published rule's five buckets left the eight `echo`s inside `_fallback_lines` over: a function body is not a construct the census can read a stream off, because where its bytes go is a property of its CALL SITES, elsewhere in the file. A **sixth** bucket now names the obligation exactly — an `echo`/`printf` inside a helper FUNCTION is disposable only by ENUMERATING that helper's call sites and showing that every one CAPTURES its output, and the enumeration is part of the disposition, not a step before it — discharged in the header for `_fallback_lines`'s **three** sites, all of them `_put_err "$( { … } )"`. Its residual is named where it is read: a helper that acquires a NEW **uncaptured** call site is invisible to the census, since the new bare call is not an `echo`/`printf` and matches no alternative in the pattern. Measured — a bare `_fallback_lines` planted at top level held the census at **31 hits with nothing left over**, no hit even naming the function, while taking a COMPLETE run with stdout on `/dev/full` from **rc 0 to rc 1** and reddening **71** assertions of `tests/gh-code-search-selftest.sh`. That split is the point: the test saw it, the rule a maintainer applies could not, and the header now says which of the two holds the property. Also corrected on this pass: the header's counted exception was off by one class — "one reachable path reaches none of the states" named only the unguarded `mktemp`, while every rc-2 usage refusal also emits zero stdout, so it now reads as one VERDICT path plus the usage refusals, which write only to stderr. **Every plant above was restored by `cp` snapshot + `cmp`, never `git checkout`/`git restore`.** Re-derived from green runs on this pass: `tests/lib-set-derivation-selftest.sh` **34** assertions (was 27), `tests/gh-code-search-selftest.sh` **272**, all **35** selftests rc 0, `shellcheck 0.9.0 -S error` clean over CI's own file expression. **⚠ For the next release PR:** this adds a bin, so `docs/UPGRADE.md` §6 owes the `PATH` symlink loop — a symlink tracks its target's *content*, not the *set* of tools — and `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check` gains it in `TOOLS`, so a seat that skips the loop gets a new warn line naming exactly that. Consumers who **vendor** must copy `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh` beside it: it sources the lib for `kb_require_value` / `kb_require_positional` / `kb_is_uint` / `kb_ere_match` rather than minting a fourth inline mirror of them, and `agent-board-toolkit-drift-check` reports a `MISSING-LIB` for a copy without it.

- **card#7038** — **`release-pr-body` stops declaring two surfaces that carry no behaviour: the `## Card coverage` section is emitted only when it actually measured, and the inert `title_prefix` config key is removed.** The coverage section used to render unconditionally and, whenever it could not check anything (no board token, no `.promote` config, or no promote tool reachable), said so — a heading whose entire content was "not checked here, run `promote-released-cards --dry-run` before merge". That is a placeholder, not a measurement: it tells the merger nothing about what the merge contains, and it was struck by hand from a release PR body, which does not hold because the next release re-emits it. **The section's presence is now the signal that coverage was measured**; where any leg of the check is missing the body carries no section at all. **The `--dry-run`-before-merge obligation did not disappear — it moved to where a reader looks for release process:** this repo's `VERSIONING.md` § Release flow, and `docs/INSTALL.md` §4 for a consumer wiring the keys up (which now also states that a body with no coverage section means the check did NOT run, rather than that it passed). A run that was attempted and failed still reports that, as before — a fact about the release, not a placeholder. **`title_prefix` is deleted from `bin/release-pr-body`, `examples/release-pr.json.example`, this repo's `.release-pr.json`, the `release-artifacts-check` config-key comment and six selftest fixtures.** It was read into a variable that nothing ever interpolated: declared to consumers as a configurable knob while being incapable of changing any output. The observable proof it was inert is that every release PR title shipped lowercase `release: vX.Y.Z` while the declared prefix said `Release`; emitting it now would silently retitle every consumer's release PRs to match a value nobody set deliberately, so it is removed rather than honoured. **Consumer impact: a `title_prefix` left in your own `.release-pr.json` is simply ignored (no error) — the key never did anything; drop it when convenient.** No test changed meaning to accommodate any of this: the three new coverage arms (no token / no promoter / no `.promote`) were **watched red on the pre-change generator**, each against a positive control that differs by exactly one leg, and the promoter-absent arm derives its `PATH` by removing the directories that carry a promoter rather than trusting the runner's — it passed for the wrong reason on a host with the toolkit installed on `PATH`, which is how that was found. One assertion was replaced rather than deleted: `has 'Not checked here' == false` in the measured arm could no longer fail in either direction, so it now asserts the heading is present *because* the check ran. **The same pass measured the class the two instances belong to** — the selftest pins these config keys' shape, not their effect — by mutating each declared key in turn and re-running: `version_file`, `version_regex`, `ref_token_regex`, `card_token_regex` and `tag_format` red, while `main_branch`, `dev_branch` and the `artifacts` checklist rendering stayed green, i.e. no test would have noticed if they stopped working. **Those three gaps are closed in this same release — see the follow-up entry below**, which is why that sentence is written in the past tense.

- **card#7038** — **the three remaining instances of the same class: `tests/release-pr-body-selftest.sh` now asserts the `artifacts` checklist's EFFECT, and stops setting `main_branch`/`dev_branch` to their own default values.** Found by mutation, not by reading — each declared config key was blanked in turn on a scratch copy and the suite re-run — and reproduced here before the fix: with `## Release artifacts` wrapped in `if false;`, or with either branch key's `cfg_opt` read deleted, the pre-change selftest passed **rc 0 with zero FAIL lines** in all three cases. **`artifacts` was a declared OUTPUT nothing asserted:** `release-artifacts-selftest.sh` covers `bin/release-artifacts-check`, the asserter, and never runs this generator at all, so the checklist render had no test anywhere. It now has one that drives the first member, a member templated mid-string, and the **last** member (so a render truncating after one entry reds rather than passing on a prefix), asserts no raw `{{version}}` survives, and carries a control with `artifacts` deleted so "emitted only when declared" is a discriminating claim rather than a section that prints unconditionally. **`main_branch`/`dev_branch` were the more dangerous sub-shape, because they looked covered:** every fixture SET them to `main`/`dev` — their own defaults — so a pass could not distinguish *the key was read* from *the default fired*. A fixture that sets a key to its own default is a control that cannot discriminate.
