The default-valued settings are removed from every fixture that carried them — here, and in the two selftests whose fixtures declared them for tools that read neither key (`release-artifacts-check`, `promote-released-cards`), where they were dead config on top of being inert (the reader is `bin/release-pr-body` alone, derived rather than recalled) — leaving the absent-key path genuinely absent and asserted through the body header; and one new fixture uses non-default names (`trunk`/`integration`) on an origin that **also carries decoy `main` and `dev` branches on their own commits and tag**, so a build that ignores the config still produces a complete rc-0 body — a wrong one — instead of merely crashing. That is what separates the two readings; non-default branch names alone would not. Both keys are asserted on effect, not presence: `main_branch` through the resolved baseline as well as the header, `dev_branch` through which commits are bundled. **Every new assertion was watched red and named the right thing** — each per-key assertion against its own single-key mutation, and the both-keys header assertion (`…and not the defaults`) against the COMBINED one, because it is the arm that stays green while either key alone is still read; the four rc lines are preconditions, not per-key coverage. The runner's own ability to red was proved the same ephemeral way the mutations were taken — a hardcoded commit count on the scratch copy, reverted after it was watched, so nothing in the tree carries it (the controls that DO ship are the config-minus-a-key arms and the decoy-branch fixture described above). **No consumer impact:** `bin/release-pr-body` is unchanged — this release closes a coverage gap, it does not change behaviour, and a `.release-pr.json` that sets `main_branch`/`dev_branch` explicitly (as `examples/release-pr.json.example` does) stays correct and is now actually exercised.

## [0.29.0] - 2026-08-18

**Minor — `kbcard` gains a board-wide `search` verb and the `field create` / `delete` / `retype` write side, `--name-file` / `--description-file` take externally-authored text from a source the caller's shell never expands, and four reads that answered an unreadable response with a confident wrong value now refuse.** 10 PRs since v0.28.0 (#247–#256): nine card-carrying, plus one docs-only `docs/UPGRADE.md` §6 entry for v0.28.0 (#247). **Minor** per `VERSIONING.md § Bump sizing` — a new `kbcard` read verb, three new `field` write verbs, and two new flags.

**⚠ UPGRADING — one action plus a re-vendor, both in [`docs/UPGRADE.md`](UPGRADE.md) §6 `v0.29.0`.** **No `bin/` tool is ADDED this release, so the `PATH` symlink loop is not owed** — every new verb and flag lands inside `kbcard`, which an existing symlink already tracks (a symlink tracks its target's *content*; it is the *set* of tools it cannot track, which is why an added bin owes the loop and this release does not). (1) **[host] `next-dl` now REFUSES (rc 1) to mint when a configured board cannot be read at all**, where it previously minted from the local `max+1` floor — a seat whose board config is stale, or whose board is unreachable, will see a refusal where it used to get a number. That number was derived from a floor the tool knew was incomplete, and a DL is a correlation key. (2) **[vendor] a broad re-vendor, and `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh` is in it** — the tolerant-parse primitive the bins now call lives in the lib, so a new bin beside an old lib fails at runtime with `kb_parse_resp: command not found`. Re-vendor the lib TOGETHER with whichever of `bin/kbcard`, `bin/adopt-to-dl`, `bin/dl-a1-register-field`, `bin/next-dl`, `bin/board-card-start`, `bin/board-snapshot`, `bin/board-stats`, `bin/dl-a0-backfill-triaged` and `bin/promote-released-cards` you copied. `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` vendor surfaces are unchanged.

- **card#6771** — **`kbcard search <query>`: the board's free-text search gets a verb, and it prints the field it matched on.** The API has always matched `name` + `description` on `GET /tasks/search.json` and returned the whole card; `kbcard` has always called that endpoint (`resolve_task`, the whole-board paginator) — just never with a caller's term. The only board-wide read VERB was `list`, whose fixed ten-field projection drops `description`, so **grepping a card body through `list` answered 0 no matter what the board held** — a confident wrong answer, not an error. `search` prints one **block per hit**: a `card <id> · stage <sid> · <name>` header line, then the card's **full description** indented two spaces on every line (`(no description)` in words where there is none, because an empty indented block reads as a rendering fault). Reusing `list`'s projection would have hidden the field the search matched on and moved the same miss one layer over, so printing it is the verb's contract; the population is the server's match set rather than the board, which is what makes unbounded free text affordable here. `--column` / `--type` filter client-side exactly as on `list` — through the **same** select, extracted as `_kbc_card_filter` at this second caller rather than copied — and carry the same `<M> of <N>` stderr denominator; a filter that removed every match says so distinctly from a query that matched nothing, and both are explicit lines at rc 0, never a silent empty stdout. The query is the endpoint's own `q=` token stream, so `name:<needle>` / `description:<needle>` and the rest of the DSL pass through, and the `--help` text now states which of the server's two free-text behaviours a caller is getting (FULLTEXT prefix-AND, or the whole-term substring fallback a short word triggers) rather than implying one rule. **Wire + refusals:** the term rides **inside** the existing `q=` value after this board's `board_id=` (a second `board_id=` token is ANDed by the parser, never swapped in — so no query can retarget the read), percent-encoded as one value through `jq`'s `@uri`, which is also why a non-ASCII term encodes to its UTF-8 bytes and not to a code point. A blank/whitespace-only query is **rc 2 before any request** (the server trims it, finds no token and answers the WHOLE board — the widest wrong answer available), and an incomplete board read is **rc 1 with nothing printed**, since a match set missing whatever was on the failed page reads as *not on the board*. **Changed in the shared lib:** `fetch_board_cards` takes an optional 5th argument, the search term — omitted, it rebuilds the historic URL byte for byte, which is asserted as a control, so the eight whole-board calls across the six consumer bins (derived from the tree, not recalled) are untouched. Two of its stderr lines are claims about a POPULATION (`an empty board`, `board has N cards`) and are now scoped: under a query they name a match set, because `meta.total` is then the match count. It gains **rc 5** — the term could not be encoded, no request issued — which is deliberately *not* folded into rc 1, whose three causes are a closed set that `next-dl`'s rc-1 arm quotes verbatim. **Tests:** **+68** assertions in `kbcard-selftest` (the renderer on synthetic cards, then the bin driven as a process over a faked API), **+21** in `kb-board-lib-selftest` (wire shape, encoding, the scoped wordings — each beside a no-query control), and **+8** in `fetch-board-cards-caller-claims-selftest`, whose registry now carries this call, its arm, the rc arity measured through the verb, and two ruled exemptions for the zero-result lines. Five mutations were watched red and restored byte-identically: dropping the description from the renderer (6 red), swallowing an incomplete fetch instead of refusing (15 red, one of them printing 200 partial cards as a complete answer), dropping the term from the URL (3 red), un-scoping the census wording (2 red), and removing the blank-query guard (3 red — which also measured that the unguarded path really does issue the whole-board request at rc 0). rc 5 is reachable from no input, so it is exercised through a `jq` stand-in that fails only the `@uri` call (`tests/_kb-jq-uri-fail-stub.sh`), with a real-jq control beside it.

- **card#6630** — **an unreadable page > 1 stops ending the board scan as if it were a short page, in both copies of the WHOLE-BOARD CARD paginator.** **Scope, stated up front because a count in an earlier draft of this entry was wrong: this shape has THREE instances in the tree, and all three are closed in this release — the two whole-board card paginators first, `bin/board-stats`'s changelog window second (its own paragraph below).** The population was derived rather than recalled — every loop that issues repeated API requests to accumulate one result set — and it is four loops: `fetch_board_cards`, `fetch_whole_board`, `bin/board-stats`'s `_bs_window_rows` (the changelog window, cursor-paged on `before=`), and `bin/_dependabot-reconcile.py`'s `gh_alerts` (paging delegated to `gh api --paginate`, which fail-closes on a non-array body and is therefore not an instance). **`_bs_window_rows` is the third instance and is closed too**, in a later change rather than this one because its failure policy was a decision and not a predicate edit — it has its own paragraph below. `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` carries the derivation and the per-instance measurements. A whole-board read pages until a page comes back short. `card#6594` made a `2xx` carrying no `.data` **array** a refusal — but only on **page 1**; on a later page it still fell back to `[]`, and `[]` is a short page, so the scan ENDED and the caller was handed a board it had only partly read. `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`'s `fetch_board_cards` returned **rc 0** with a truncated array and an empty stderr; `bin/promote-released-cards`'s `fetch_whole_board` — a MOVER that PATCHes cards — returned the same truncated list and promoted from it. The envelope predicate now applies to **every** page, at each copy's existing failure policy: **rc 2** in the lib, the rc its contract already assigns to *a page > 1 failed mid-pagination* (the same rc the transport and non-2xx arms return for the same page — the page selects the rc, not the cause), and a `die` in the mirror, reached BEFORE the short-read census because *page 2 was unreadable* and *the board is bigger than what arrived* are different operator actions.

  **The two PAGE-1 predicates are deliberately still different, and converging them would have been the wrong fix.** The mirror dies on zero **cards**, which a mover can afford because a board with nothing to promote is never a working state for it; a read verb cannot, because `kbcard list` on a genuinely empty board must succeed. Neither page-1 predicate moved: an empty envelope on page 1 still dies in the mirror and still succeeds at rc 0 in the lib. **⚠ ONE PAGE-1 ACCEPTANCE IN THE MIRROR DID MOVE, and it is stated on its own line rather than as an aside about voice**, because the predicate is what EXTRACTS `data` and page 1 goes through it too. For the two shapes `.data // []` passed through as a non-array — `{"data":{"id":9}}` and `{"data":"str"}` — **`fetch_whole_board` used to return rc 0 with EMPTY stdout, and it now dies at rc 2.** Measured against `git show HEAD:bin/promote-released-cards` with the real function lifted onto the selftest's page stub. The accumulator's `jq -s 'add'` did fault (status 5, `array ([]) and object ({"id":9}) cannot be added`) but that fault is **non-fatal**: `errexit` is not inherited by a command-substitution subshell and nothing in this repo sets `inherit_errexit`, so `CARDS="$(fetch_whole_board)"` took an empty card list at rc 0 and the run continued, aborting three assignments later at `MISSING="$(jq -n --argjson md "$MATCHED_DLS" …)"` with `jq: invalid JSON text passed to --argjson` — a different jq, at a different site, at status 2. The *process* exit was 2 either way and no card was PATCHed either way (both aborts land before the move loop), which is precisely what made an earlier draft of this entry describe it as a change of voice; that was wrong, and the draft is retracted here. No other page-1 shape moved, measured by running the same lift against `HEAD` and against the fix over six page-1 bodies: `{"error":…}`, `{"data":null}`, `{"data":[]}` and an unparseable `<html>` body all reached the zero-cards die at rc 2 before this change and still do; only those two rows moved, and the page-1 PREDICATE (zero cards) is untouched. Pinned in `tests/promote-pagination-selftest.sh`. That refusal's message names board membership as the likely cause, which is wrong for these two shapes; it was already wrong for `{"error":…}` and `{"data":null}`, this change adds exactly two shapes to its reach, and rewording an operator-facing refusal is ask-gated — so the acceptance is recorded on card#6630 in `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` rather than widened here.

  **⚠ "a server that declares `meta.total` is unaffected" is measurably FALSE, and that correction is the reason this is not cosmetic here.** The `meta.total` census did catch this case at **rc 4** — but rc 4 EMITS its partial array, and the consumers that render on 3|4 rendered it. Measured through the shipped bins against a page 1 of 200 rows with `meta.total: 201` and a page 2 of `{"error":…}`: `board-snapshot` printed a board line derived from the truncated read (`• Board 5 (kanban-board): in-flight 0`) with the `INCOMPLETE` warning on a stderr its SessionStart display does not show; it now prints `(board read failed — fetch rc=2)`. For the refuse-policy consumers (`kbcard list`, `next-dl`, `dl-a0-backfill-triaged`, `board-card-start`, `kbcard field`'s census) the disposition is unchanged on such a server — only the rc in their message moves, 4 → 2 — and on a server that OMITS `meta.total` the answer moves from a silent truncated one to a refusal — measured end-to-end on the shipped `kbcard list`, which printed a **200-card list at rc 0** against that fixture and now exits 1 naming `fetch rc=2`. The other consumers are not re-measured here and the claim is not extended to them by assertion: each already had a registered rc-2 disposition, which `tests/fetch-board-cards-caller-claims-selftest.sh` derives and pins, and this change adds no rc for any of them to have missed.

  **THE THIRD INSTANCE — `bin/board-stats`'s changelog window, the same defect reached through the loop's END-OF-LOG signal instead of its short-page one.** `_BS_PAGE_JQ` opened with `(.data // []) as $d`, and jq's `//` substitutes its right-hand side for `false` and `null` as well as for absent — so a `2xx` carrying a proxy's error object arrived as `n: 0`, and `n < _BS_CL_LIMIT` is precisely how this loop decides the LOG IS EXHAUSTED. The window ended there with `truncated=false` and `error=null`, and the report rendered flow counts off a truncated window with **no `⚠` line and no `(WINDOW TRUNCATED)` marker**, at rc 0 — a plausible wrong answer for a rate question. **The live set is narrower than a `.data`-shape test would suggest and is stated as measured, not as reasoned:** driving the real `_bs_window_rows` with page 1 = 200 rows, exactly `{"error":…}`, `{"data":null}` and `{"data":false}` were silent — the three values `//` substitutes for — while every other unreadable shape (`{"data":"str"}`, `{"data":{"id":9}}`, `{"data":{}}`, `[{"error":…}]`, `<html>502</html>`) already faulted the page parse at `$d[-1]` and was caught. `{"data":false}` silent vs `{"data":{}}` caught is the discriminating pair, and both are now selftest legs. **The fix is the same envelope predicate, `(.data|type) == "array"`, and it adds nothing:** a body carrying no row array makes that program emit **nothing**, which is already what a jq fault produces at this call site, so it lands on the existing `[[ -n "$page" ]]` guard and the per-page `error` the loop already returns — no new exit code, no new field, no new message, and rc is unchanged (a partial report is still rc 0, which is this tool's contract). **Unlike the two card paginators, there is no page split here, and that is a difference in the functions and not in the ruling:** each of those has two documented rcs to select between, while this one has a single `error` channel that `_bs_one_board` already renders identically for any `pages > 0`. Page 1 therefore moves the same way and by the same predicate — an unreadable page 1 used to produce a flow section of zeros that read as a quiet board and now carries the `⚠`. **`truncated` is deliberately NOT set by this path**: it stays a property of the loop's three STOPS (the cutoff, the page cap, a log that ends inside the window), while an aborted read reports through `error`, which `_bs_one_board` renders as `changelog window INCOMPLETE … the flow counts below are a floor, not a total` above the counts it qualifies. A genuinely exhausted log answers `{"data":[]}` — an array — and still stops the loop silently and correctly at every page number, which is why the predicate is a TYPE test and not a truthiness one. **This is also what makes `bin/board-stats`' own header claim true again** — *"a number that is a floor rather than a total (a capped card read, a truncated changelog window) says so where it is printed"* — which the silent path falsified. It is repaired by the code rather than reworded, and the selftest asserts it on the **rendered text** a human reads, not on the JSON field. **Coverage:** `tests/board-stats-selftest.sh` gains 22 assertions, and every one of them was watched to fail before it was trusted — nine separate mutations, because the controls are built to survive the obvious one: reverting the predicate reds the refusal legs but leaves the `{"data":[]}` and `{"data":{}}` controls green, which is the point of them. Each mutation asserts a **unique** match on its target text before it is applied and prints the applied diff, since a scripted mutation that landed on the wrong line and came back green is a failure this card has already recorded twice.

  **Consumer impact.** Both changed files are co-vendored (`bin/_kb-board-lib.sh` and the standalone `bin/promote-released-cards`), so a vendor-by-copy consumer must re-vendor to get this. No flag, no new rc, no interface change: rc 2 and the mirror's `die` both predate this change. `bin/board-stats` is vendorable too and must be re-vendored for the third fix. `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` vendor surfaces are unchanged. **Coverage:** `tests/kb-board-lib-selftest.sh` and `tests/promote-pagination-selftest.sh` each gain a page-2 block covering both server shapes (declares / omits `meta.total`), the quiet-mode path, and TWO controls that pass under the old code and the new — a legitimate short page 2 and an empty page 2 must still complete the read, because a predicate that cannot tell those from an unreadable body refuses every board over one page. Every refusal-side assertion was watched to fail against the pre-fix predicate (9 of them in the lib suite, 5 in the mirror's) before it was watched to pass.

- **card#6631** — **`next-dl` refuses to mint when a CONFIGURED board cannot be read at all, instead of minting from the local floor.** The offline `max+1` fallback takes its maximum over local `## DL-NNN` headers **and** the board's stamped `dl_number`s, and it already refused on paginator rc 2, 3 and 4 — a read that was not whole. rc 1 was the exception: *nothing* of the board was read (no response, a non-2xx status, or a 2xx carrying no card array), so the one branch that fail-softed handed back a number derived from a floor it knew was incomplete. It now exits **1** with the reason on stderr. No ranking among the four rcs is claimed, here or in the code — an earlier draft called rc 1's undercount the maximal one, and that is false against rc 2, which emits **nothing** as well (only rc 3 and rc 4 emit a partial array), so a fail-soft there would have undercounted just as totally. Nothing rides on it now that the policy is uniform. **⚠ UPGRADING — this is a narrowing, and it removes a promise the tool used to make:** `next-dl`'s header advertised that it never blocked allocation offline; offline allocation is **withdrawn as a contract**, not narrowed, so a machine with a configured board and no route to it no longer mints. That is a deliberate trade, and the reasoning is recorded with the decision in [`docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md`](CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md): an allocator that answers when it cannot see the board answers from a floor it knows is incomplete, and a DL is a correlation key — a duplicate one silently merges two unrelated work items. The measured downstream is what the fail-soft cost: driven through `adopt-to-dl` against a board answering `200` with `<html>502</html>` and a local floor of `DL-0300`, the old path printed `skipping board check` and then **stamped DL-0301 on the card**; the new one aborts before any stamp. **Two stated bounds, deliberately not closed here, and the line between them and the refusal is resolve-failure vs fetch-failure — NOT "a board is configured" vs "no board is configured":** a **`resolve_board_cfg` failure**, where no request is issued at all (no `curl`/`jq`, an unreadable or absent board env, no `api_base` for `kb_resolve_env` to publish, a board env that sets `KBCARD_API`, a missing or unreadable token file, or a resolved config with no board id — measured, each still mints), and a **complete, successful read of a board carrying no `dl_number` stamp**, where `max_int`'s `grep` matches nothing and `pipefail` exits `board_dl_max` down the same branch. Both still mint from the header scan alone (unchanged; the board-less case is the path `docs/INSTALL.md` §3b and `examples/kanban-board.env.example` already describe), and **a named, reachable board reaches either** — so `1 = no usable board config` is not an accurate reading of that exit code. `bin/next-dl`'s header and its `board_dl_max` exit-code note own the enumeration, and name two further known-open cases (a board the token cannot see, and a `2xx` whose `.data` is an array of non-cards) on which a mint proceeds past a board that answered `200`; both are the envelope-predicate class of **card#6630**, not this arm's policy. The **atomic claim endpoint is untouched** — it is the race-free primary path. Which endpoint gates this whole branch depends on the mode: the claim by default, and under `--peek` the non-consuming inspect endpoint, which is the only one that mode consults (measured — with the claim endpoint answering a healthy `200`, `--peek` against a 404 inspect endpoint reaches the offline branch and never issues a claim). Either way the branch is reached when the endpoint **that mode consulted** returned `dl_sequence_call`'s rc 1 — absent, unreachable, or a 2xx carrying no usable value. Fixing rc 1 needed **no** new paginator return code: the three causes stay one rc, so the paginator's returned codes are unchanged, the other five consumer bins are untouched, and `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`'s only edits are comments (the rc-1 contract line, which no longer implies the DL minter fail-softs there, and the parse-site note that named this residual). That retires the forward-looking note in the **card#6594** entry below (*"refusing on all of them would break `next-dl` on every offline or board-less checkout, so the remedy needs the causes separated first"*), on two different grounds: the **offline** half is withdrawn by the ruling (that breakage is now the intended behaviour), while the **board-less** half was never true — a board-less checkout fails at `resolve_board_cfg` and never reaches a paginator rc at all, so no rc-1 policy could affect it. Measured: `next-dl --board <unconfigured>` still mints from the local floor at rc 0 under this change. `tests/next-dl-selftest.sh` gains the refusal legs plus two controls that make them able to fail — a readable board on the same fixture still mints `DL-0301`, and an unconfigured board still mints — and `tests/fetch-board-cards-caller-claims-selftest.sh`'s registry carries the reworded arm.
- **card#6648** — **`kbcard` gains `--name-file` / `--description-file`, and one text-argument resolver behind all three free-text pairs.** `--description "…"` forced externally-authored text to be typed inline, where the **caller's shell expands it before `kbcard` is started**: a `$(…)` or a backtick sitting in a report someone else wrote runs as a command on the invoking box, and `argv` already holds its output by the time anything in this tool can look. That is why the fix is a **source that never meets a shell** and not a validator — an in-tool `$(`-sniffing check would be a check incapable of detecting the thing it names, since the expansion happened in another process. Reproduced with a discriminating control before anything was written (`bash -c 'echo "pre-$(id -u)-post"'` → expanded; the single-quoted control → literal; `--description "$(cat f)"` → byte-exact, because a command substitution's result is not re-scanned), so a safe path already existed — what was wrong is that it depended on a caller remembering a wrapper, every time, on text they did not write. **Added:** `--name-file PATH` and `--description-file PATH` on **`create-card`** and **`patch`**, each mutually exclusive with its inline twin (`rc 2`), each refusing a missing, unreadable, `-` or text-free (empty / whitespace-only) file at **`rc 2` before any request**, and each sending the file's bytes with interior newlines and leading whitespace verbatim, trailing newlines trimmed and CRLF folded to LF. **Changed:** `_kbc_comment_content` is hoisted into **`_kbc_text_arg <outvar> <flag> <require-verb> <inline> <file>`**, which `--content`, `--name` and `--description` all route through — a second near-copy of that contract for `--description` would have been the defect, not the feature. Its one policy parameter is `<require-verb>` (mechanism in the primitive, policy at the caller — `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` ground rule 5): non-empty names the verb whose whole request the text is, so the pair is **required** and its **inline** value is normalized and blank-checked too (`comment`'s shipped contract, carried over byte for byte and asserted by the unchanged comment block of `tests/kbcard-selftest.sh`); empty makes the pair an **optional field setter**, where neither source given stays silent and the inline value — a flag that predates its file twin — rides through **verbatim**. `-` is now refused by name on **all three** file flags: it already failed (`[[ -r - ]]` is false), so only the message changes, never the acceptance. `create-card`'s name refusal now reads `--name or --name-file required`. **Deliberately NOT built, and recorded rather than implied:** the new flags do **not** require an absolute path, and neither does `--content-file` — what makes the file form safe is that the text never passes through a shell, which a relative path delivers exactly as well; absolute-required is ergonomics, retrofitting it onto the shipped flag would narrow what an existing caller may pass, and applying it to only the new flags would leave one tool with two rules for one thing (the divergence this consolidation exists to remove). Making the file form **mandatory** for `--description` is likewise not built: it removes an accepted input. Both are one gated change across all three flags if they are ever wanted. **Consumer impact: additive.** No existing invocation changes rc, body or output — the inline flags accept exactly what they accepted and put exactly what they put on the wire, asserted by dedicated controls (a whitespace-only `--description` still writes; an inline CRLF value still reaches the wire unnormalized) so that a later "harmonization" cannot narrow them silently. **Coverage:** `tests/kbcard-selftest.sh` gains 134 assertions across both verbs × both new flags — mutual exclusion, missing path, an unreadable-but-present path (a directory: `-r` yet uncattable, chosen over `chmod 000` because a root runner reads that anyway and a check that cannot fail is a decoration), `-`, empty and whitespace-only files, trailing-newline trimming, CRLF normalization, and the load-bearing one: **a file whose content carries `$(id -u)` and backticks arrives byte-exact in the request body**, with the unexpanded substitution asserted as text so the equality cannot pass on an expanded body. Watched red under four mutations — routing the file read through `eval` (the wire body came back carrying this box's uid and the output of `date`), deleting the `-` refusal, blank-checking the untrimmed value, and inverting the inline-mode knob (which reds `comment`'s inline contract *and* the optional setters' acceptance controls, i.e. the parameter is asserted in both directions). **Docs:** the `kbcard` usage header gains the free-text convention as its opening paragraph — where an agent reading `kbcard` with no arguments actually meets it — and `comment`'s block now points at it instead of restating it; README gains a `§ kbcard free-text flags` section that owns the contract for all three pairs, with the comment bullet pointing there. `_kb-board-lib.sh`, `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` are untouched.
- **card#6661** — **the whole-board paginator stops destroying the caller's own stderr log.** Under `KB_FETCH_LOUD` — which `bin/kbcard` sets, so `list`, `field delete` and `field retype --restamp-dl` all reach it — `fetch_board_cards` (`bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`) sent curl's stderr to the **path** `/dev/stderr`. Redirecting to that path **re-opens** the file behind the caller's fd 2 rather than duplicating the descriptor, and a caller whose stderr is a **regular file** — `kbcard list 2>run.log`, which is how an agent logs — has it re-opened `O_TRUNC` **on every page fetch**. Everything written before the first one is gone, and the caller's *next* write then lands at its own unchanged offset, so the file reads back as a NUL-filled hole followed by the tail: a log of plausible length whose head is missing. Measured through the real function against the reverted fix: **28 bytes of caller log destroyed, 10 NUL bytes, 39 bytes on disk where 57 were written.** Clean on a pipe and on a tty, where `O_TRUNC` is a no-op. That is NOT why the suite missed it, and the distinction matters to whoever adds the next case: every pre-existing `fetch_board_cards` case here already redirects to a **regular file** and was in fact truncated on every call — nothing was lost only because none of them WRITES to its stderr file before the first fetch. The target is now a **descriptor** — fd 2 itself when loud, a loop-scoped `9>/dev/null` when quiet — so nothing is re-opened and no `{fd}` allocation is relied on (the repo declares no bash floor; this spelling needs no version feature, and bash saves and restores a caller's own fd 9 around the loop's redirect, so the number cannot collide). Note for anyone tempted by the one-line version: re-spelling the variable does **not** work — with `errsink='&2'`, `2>"$errsink"` creates a literal file named `&2` in the cwd and swallows the stream entirely. **The quiet default is unchanged** (`board-snapshot`'s fail-soft SessionStart display still sees curl's stderr go to `/dev/null`), and no rc, message or stdout moves. `tests/kb-board-lib-selftest.sh` gains eight assertions on the caller log's **content, byte count and NUL count** — the last because a command substitution silently drops NUL bytes, so a string compare alone cannot see the corruption.
- **card#6525** — **`kbcard field` gains `create` / `delete` / `retype` — and `retype` is a THIN CALL on the server's atomic type-conversion route, not a client-side migration.** The API now owns the conversion end to end (`POST /api/v3/custom_fields/{id}/change-type.json`): the definition **and** every stored `tasks.payload` value keyed by it move in **one transaction**, with the key's search index rebuilt inside it and **no delete**, so `placeholder`, `mask`, `multiline`, `is_filterable` and `position` survive — which a delete-and-recreate could not do. So this verb issues **no delete, no recreate, no per-card cast**: an earlier cut of this card implemented the client-side sequence (enumerate → cast-plan → capture → DELETE → recreate → restamp → verify) with a cast matrix, a pre-delete plan, a stranded-board report and a printed finishing command, and **all of it is deleted** rather than kept in step. There is nothing left here for a crash to leave half-migrated, and the root cause the sequence worked around was never the CLI's to fix.
  **What the two verbs it does NOT replace still guard.** Two server behaviours were re-measured on the sandbox instance while building this, and they are why `delete` is fail-closed — not why `retype` exists: **(A)** deleting a definition does **not** cascade to `tasks.payload` — resident values survive, orphaned, and are **never revalidated**, so after a create over the same key they still carry whatever JSON type they were written with; **(B)** deleting **purges** the key's `task_custom_field_values` side-table rows and creating does **not** rebuild them — only a payload **write** touching the key re-indexes it. Composed: a hand-rolled delete-then-create "type migration" leaves the definition saying `string`, the payload still holding a JSON number, and `custom_field_<key>:…` returning **0 cards** — measured end to end on the sandbox, where after `delete --orphan-values` + `create` the value read back verbatim (`"DL-0001"`) while the same DSL filter that had matched the card returned **`{"n":0}`**. `retype` never touches that path. **Four in-file claims that the rewrite falsified were left standing and are now corrected** — a *retype* grep reaches none of them, they were found by re-reading the subsystem: the lifecycle section header still said *create and delete are the primitives; retype COMPOSES them*, with *exactly one POST site and one DELETE site in this file*, both false and both contradicted by the `field retype` section 190 lines below (it now points at that section rather than restating it — a restatement that has to be kept in step is the defect, not the drift); `field create`'s option-flattening comment still justified itself by *`retype`, which has real labels to carry across*, which retype stopped doing when it began building `map({value: .})` itself; and one of four sibling copies of *a key is unique per board AND its type is IMMUTABLE* survived the cut that deleted the clause from the other three.
  **`field retype --field <name|id> --to <type> [--options a,b,c] [--accept-ref-impact] [--restamp-dl]`.** The wire contract was read out of the server's own source, not recalled (`CustomFieldsController::changeType`, `CustomFieldMutator::changeType`, `CustomFieldTypeCast`, `CustomFieldTypeConversionException`): the route takes exactly `type`, `options`, `from_type` and `acknowledge_ref_impact`.
  - **There is no dry run and none is simulated.** `--check` is a mutator parameter the controller never passes — it exists only on `php artisan kanban:change-custom-field-type` — so **no `--check` / `--dry-run` flag was added**. **Preview-by-refusal** is what the API offers: the whole board is scanned before anything is written, so a blocked conversion answers **422** naming **every** offending card with its `category` and the validator's own message, and leaves the board byte-identical. The refusal **is** the preflight, and the help text says so rather than implying a preview the API cannot serve.
  - **`from_type` is always sent**, making the compare-and-swap free rather than optional: resolving `--field` already reads the current type (there is no per-field GET, so the board index is read either way). A definition whose type moved between that read and this write is its **own** outcome — **412**, *the field's type changed under you*, naming both sides, nothing written — never folded in with a 422.
  - **`--options` is a passthrough; the client stopped having an opinion.** `assertOptionsMatchTarget` already rules that a scalar target carrying options is a 422, that an option target from a non-option source **requires** them (*never inherited from the stored column*), and that they are carried over verbatim between `enum` and `multi_select` — and it re-derives all of that under the row lock. The client-side `takes_options` gate, the carry-the-existing-options-across capture and the `[]`-blind emptiness guard are therefore **deleted**, not re-spelled; the comma list is turned into the server's `[{value}]` shape and sent, and the server's refusals are surfaced verbatim. Two selftest legs pin exactly that: a scalar target carrying `--options`, and an option target with none, must both **reach the wire** and be answered in the **server's** words. **A third client-side rule survived that cut, and the passthrough claim now names it as the exception it is:** the shared CSV validator refuses a DUPLICATE value at **rc 2** with a rationale imported from `set-options` (*a converge-to-set target must be distinct*), while `assertOptionsMatchTarget` has **no uniqueness rule at all** — it validates the array, the value's presence/type/length and an optional label, nothing more, so `--options a,a` would be accepted on the wire. The other two rules the validator carries — at least one value, no empty value — ARE the server's own (`options => array|min:1`, `options.*.value => required`), coincident by derivation rather than by luck, so only the duplicate refusal is a second ruling. **Removing it from `retype`'s path would change what that verb ACCEPTS**, which is an rc-contract decision the callers own and not a cleanup, so it is **recorded as a recommendation and not made here**; what changed is that the refusal, `--help`, README and this entry all state that it is kbcard's rule and not the API's — a caller refused by it is no longer sent to argue with the server about a rule the server does not have. Selftest legs pin the exception and its counterpart: the duplicate refusal must name its owner, and the empty-value refusal beside it must NOT. **⚠ And `--options` between the two option types DESTROYS the definition's explicit labels.** The server replaces the options column outright (`$locked->options = $options`) and a comma list carries no labels, so each value ends up with `label = value`; the deleted `_kbc_field_finish_cmd` used to warn about exactly this and nothing in the thin-call path did, while a selftest leg **asserted the lossy end state**. The run now names each dropped label off the definition it had already read — **no extra request** — and says that omitting `--options` carries the set over verbatim. It is printed AFTER the 2xx and in the past tense: on a refusal nothing was written and the labels are still there, so a pre-flight warning would be its own false claim.
  - **`--accept-ref-impact` is the second half of a two-call handshake and never skips the first call.** The one opt-in-able refusal is a conversion that would **move** a card's external-reference correlation. The un-acknowledged POST is always made; its 422 reports the set under `meta.ref_impact_task_ids`; only then does a second POST acknowledge **exactly that set**. The server re-derives the set under the row lock and refuses one that grew or changed, so acknowledging a guessed or carried-over set would either be refused or — if it happened to match — would launder the one gate that makes a correlation move deliberate. Without the flag the ids and the server's message print, nothing is written, rc is non-zero. Pinned by the two assertions that carry it: the **first** body acknowledges nothing, and the **second** carries exactly what the first call reported. **The two `meta` keys are capped differently** — `meta.offenders` at `CustomFieldTypeChangeResult::MAX_REPORTED_OFFENDERS` (50), `meta.ref_impact_task_ids` not at all — and this path prints from the capped one while acknowledging the uncapped one, without ever reaching `_kbc_field_change_type_report`'s truncation notice; at 200 ref-impact cards the operator saw 50 and agreed to 200, against the flag's own stated property. The disclosure is derived from the two counts and printed here.
  - **`--restamp-dl` survives, re-implemented as a post-conversion pass** — because the conversion alone leaves this card's own migration half-done. The cast matrix's text rule for a `number` source is `json_encode`, so the stored `1` becomes `"1"`, **not** `"DL-0001"`. Correlation is not at risk (the server canonicalizes `DL-028` / `28` / `#28` to one key), but a custom-field DSL filter written as `DL-0001` **string-misses** a card storing `"1"`. Each populated card's value is rewritten through **`kb_dl_canon`** — the one canonical renderer `next-dl` and `adopt-to-dl` also stamp through, never a second `DL-%04d` — and **verified by re-reading the card**. A card already holding the canonical form is left **unwritten**, so a re-run costs zero writes. ⚠ **The pass's population is SMALLER than the conversion's, and NO run of it can say the field is done.** `CustomFieldMutator::candidateQuery` is `withTrashed()->where(board_id)->whereNotNull(payload->key)` — no archive filter, no soft-delete filter — so every archived and every trashed carrier IS converted, while the census reads `/tasks/search.json`, which applies the default `SoftDeletes` scope **and** `whereNull(archived_at)` and returns neither. Left uncompared, the run printed *N of N card value(s) canonicalized and verified* and exited **0** while an arbitrary number of cards permanently held `"1"` instead of `"DL-0001"` — and un-archiving any one of them puts a non-canonical value back into the live set. The server's own `meta.converted_task_count` is now compared against the census, and a run whose conversion touched more reports that remainder as its **own outcome** — naming the count and what those carriers are — at rc 1. **That compare is one-directional, and treating it as two-way was the same defect a second time.** It can PROVE a remainder and can never rule one out, for two independent reasons: it subtracts two COUNTS taken at two different moments rather than comparing sets, so a card entering the live set between the conversion and the census masks an archived carrier one for one (2 converted live + 3 archived, 3 new live carriers, `len` 5 = `n_conv` 5, remainder 0); and the server answers a target type equal to the current one as an explicit **no-op** — `CustomFieldTypeChangeResult::noop()` hardcodes `convertedTaskCount: 0` and `typeGates` returns it **before any scan runs** — so on the re-run this tool's own remediation lines prescribe, `n_conv > len` is structurally false whatever the board holds, and the empty-census arm said *no card carries `<key>`*, which is a statement about the board this pass never reads. So **every line that could be read as a completeness claim now carries the census scope unconditionally**, in one shared wording that also names which of the two states the run is in (the server scanned and the count is a floor; or the server scanned nothing and there was no population to compare against). Whether the server **scanned** is carried into the pass as an explicit signal rather than re-inferred from `converted_task_count == 0`, because a real conversion of zero carriers and a no-op that scanned nothing are two different boards. The explicit `unseen > 0` report is untouched — it is the *stronger* statement, made only where the pass can prove it. The remainder is **reported, never chased**: widening the census to `?archived=1` / `?trashed=1` and PATCHing soft-deleted rows is a behaviour change with a real trade-off, and it is not this pass's to make. **The pass's own remediation lines carried the mirror-image overclaim and are now scoped to the buckets a re-run actually repairs.** *Re-running this exact command finishes the rest* — and its sibling *finishes the VISIBLE rest* on the out-of-census arm — were false whenever a stored value is **not a DL number**: `kb_dl_canon` refuses the same value identically on every pass, so no re-run has ever written one, and when that is the ONLY bucket left the line told the operator to run a command that provably does nothing, at the one moment they most needed to be told the **value** is what has to change. Both arms now speak through one `_kbc_field_rerun_note`, which names the finishable count (`patch_failed` + `mismatch` + `unread`) rather than *the rest*, says plainly that a re-run would change **nothing** when that count is zero, and carves out the non-DL values **only when there are any** — a named exception that did not occur is the same defect one size down, and is exactly what made the offender-cap notice's *fix these and re-run* wrong for the class it fires on. That conditionality is deliberately the opposite of the census-scope note's unconditionality, and the two are not to be harmonised: the census scope holds on **every** run of this pass on every board, while a non-DL remainder is **measured** per run. A third site had the same shape and is corrected with them — the unreadable-conversion-echo arm promised a re-run *finishes any `--restamp-dl` pass* while holding **no census at all**. It now says the re-run **runs** the pass this run skipped and that whether it finishes is that pass's own reported outcome — **and it says it only where `--restamp-dl` was actually passed.** The first rewrite of that arm made the sentence unconditional, which is the defect this whole scoping exists to remove, minted one size down again: on a `retype` *without* the flag there was no pass to skip and a re-run runs none either, so the replacement was strictly stronger than the hedged wording it replaced — and false, where the hedged one had merely been vacuous. The restamp half of the sentence, and the non-DL-value clause only that pass can reach, are now emitted on the flagged branch alone; the unflagged branch says the conversion is UNCONFIRMED and that the re-run is safe, and stops there. **It shipped because that branch had no test leg** — all **five** assertions that round added to the arm were taken with the flag PASSED, and the one mutation it aimed at the arm (restoring the *finishes any* wording) could only exercise that same branch, so nothing in the round was able to observe the new claim being false. The missing branch now has its own leg. No exit code changed at any of the three sites, or at this correction: the arm keeps its rc 1. Seven outcomes, reported as the seven different boards they are: **canonicalized** · **already canonical** · **PATCH failed** (never counted as verified — the pass-1 defect that must not come back — and equally never reported as *unwritten*: `kb_api` returns 1 for a timed-out `PATCH` exactly as for a refused one, and a timed-out write may already be committed, so the arm claims UNCERTAINTY and names the idempotent re-run) · **read back different** · **the verification read could not observe it** (a failed GET and a `2xx` carrying no readable value are the same non-observation, and the earlier cut reported the second as "read back a DIFFERENT value", i.e. a claim about a board nothing had read) · **not a DL number at all** · **outside this pass's census** (the remainder above). Requires `--to string`.
  - **Every other status is its own outcome**, with the server's body echoed on stderr and appended to the kbcard failure log: **413** the conversion is over one of the server's **two** resource rails — and the client names *neither*, because they measure different populations (`max_board_tasks` counts the board's cards at `CustomFieldMutator:301`; `max_examined_carriers` counts the cards holding a value for **this field** at `:513`), so "the board is over the card-scan rail" is a false statement about a 6000-card board refused by the second; the server's own message names which rail and carries the operator-supervised escape hatch, which is why the raw body is echoed rather than paraphrased — **404** the definition was deleted since this run read the field index, with **no mechanism claimed** (implicit route-model binding 404s *before* `changeType` is entered, while the mutator's re-read under the row lock throws the same 404 from inside the transaction; nothing written either way, and the client cannot tell them apart) — **000** a transport failure, which is now an **UNKNOWN** outcome and not a safe one: `kb_api_status` maps *every* non-zero `curl` exit to `000`, including `--max-time` (28) and a reset mid-response, and the lib's own header states that a timed-out POST/PATCH is **ambiguous** — the run therefore says it cannot tell whether the conversion landed and names the re-run that resolves it, in ONE shared wording it and the restamp pass's failed `PATCH` both emit — and a **`2xx` nothing can be read out of** an **UNCONFIRMED** conversion at rc 1 that deliberately does **not** go on to restamp: the write landed and this run cannot say what it landed as.
  - **A 422 is a THREE-way, not the server's documented two — found on the live surface, not in the fixtures.** The conversion's own refusals both carry `meta` and are told apart by the presence of `meta.offenders` (offender refusal vs **pair** refusal — no conversion exists between those two types at all, decided before a single task row was read, so no card is at fault). The option RULES are a plain Laravel `ValidationException` and render `{message, errors}` with **no `meta` at all** — measured against the sandbox instance, where an omitted `--options` on a `string → enum` answered exactly that. Discriminating on `meta.offenders` alone therefore reported that whole third class as a **pair** refusal, i.e. told the operator that a conversion the server *does* define does not exist, and sent them to fix the wrong thing. A refusal whose `meta.offenders` is **empty** is a real body, not a degenerate one — `assertOffendersClearOrAcknowledged` passes the scan's own (empty) list through when an acknowledgement is checked against a scan that derived **no** ref impact, reachable from `--accept-ref-impact` when the impact disappears between this run's two calls — so the offender arm no longer renders it as *0 card value(s) block this conversion*, which sent the operator looking for cards that do not exist on a run whose fault is the request. `meta` present is what says the refusal came from the conversion at all; the fixture that had invented a `meta` the server never sends is what let the defect pass a green suite, so it was corrected first and the three checks that now pin the class were **watched red** against it before the code was touched. **The offender-cap notice gave advice that was false for the class it most often fires on:** *"Fix these and re-run to see the rest"* — but a ref-impact refusal has nothing to fix (the offender is a correlation you acknowledge, not a bad value) and re-running never widens the list, which the `--accept-ref-impact` path's own sibling already stated correctly. It now branches on `meta.ref_impact_task_ids`, which is present **only** on a ref-impact refusal and whose presence also means every *blocking* offender is one (`assertOffendersClearOrAcknowledged` throws the value refusal first whenever `blocking > |ref_impact_ids|`), so the branch is exact: the ref-impact tail says there is nothing to fix and points at the uncapped id set printed two lines below, the value tail keeps the fix-then-re-run advice and adds the rule it used to contradict. **Neither tail promises a re-run finishes anything, and neither mentions `--restamp-dl`** — a 422 is a refusal, so *nothing was written*: there is no conversion half to answer as a server-side no-op, and no pass was skipped. The ref-impact tail says the refusal is acknowledged rather than repaired and points at the uncapped id set below it; the value tail says to fix the named cards and re-run, whereupon the next refusal names the next batch. (This bullet previously carried a *finishes the job (the conversion half answers as a server-side no-op)* sentence — minted by this card's own conversion-route rewrite and **already wrong then**, since a 422 lands nothing there is a no-op of — and a later round replaced it with a `--restamp-dl` clause, grafting a claim about the unreadable-**2xx** arm onto a **refusal** path whose code arms name that flag nowhere. Both described a write that did not happen; both are removed.) The call goes through `kb_api_status` rather than `kb_api` for two load-bearing reasons — five statuses are five different boards, and the handshake must **read** a 422's body, which `kb_api` can only send to stderr.
  **`field delete --field <name|id> [--orphan-values]`** is fail-closed on the populated-card census: the `N of M board cards carry <key>` denominator goes to stderr **always** (so a census that *ran* and returned zero is distinguishable from one that never ran — **not** from "the field is unpopulated", which that number cannot establish; see the third fix round below), a populated field **refuses at rc 2** naming (A) and (B) and pointing at `retype` — which no longer owns a delete/recreate sequence, and the message now says what it does own — and `--orphan-values` is the explicit opt-in that still warns loudly. **Every incomplete-read rc `fetch_board_cards` defines — 1, 2, 3 and 4 — refuses at rc 1**, because the dangerous shape is a truncated read whose *visible* cards carry nothing and therefore reads exactly like a safe delete; the four refusals are held by a positive control that deletes at rc 0 off the **same board and the same zero numerator** with a complete read. `_kbc_field_delete_call`'s two UNVERIFIED boards — a `2xx` that removed nothing (the definition is still there, and a create over that key would 422) and a `2xx` whose confirming re-read failed (assume it LANDED; the server said it acted) — were live behaviour with **zero** coverage, their fake knobs present but assigned by no leg, alongside a third such knob on the create POST's refusal. All three now have legs. The verb collapses the delete's two to one rc, which is correct at ITS boundary (a delete that did not verify is one outcome there) and is why the **message**, not the status, is what tells them apart in those legs — and the primitive's header no longer claims the three arms *cannot share one rc* while its only caller shares one.
  **`field create --key <k> --label <l> --type <t> [--options a,b,c]`** prints the **created field id** (the write verification; a 2xx carrying no id is an **unverified write** at rc 1, never a printed `null`), sends the server's `[{value}]` option shape, requires `--options` for `enum`/`multi_select` and refuses it on every other type (stored-but-never-interpreted, the same silent no-effect `set-options` refuses), and refuses an existing key at **rc 2 with zero POSTs** — a key is unique per board, so creating over the top is not a re-type. The `--field` resolver and the option-CSV validator are **hoisted out of `set-options`** into shared owners rather than copied a third time (`set-options` is behaviourally byte-equivalent; its selftest is unchanged apart from the sub-verb list in the dispatch message).
  **Third fix round — two claims this tool made about the server that the server does not honour.** Both were verified at server source (`CustomFieldMutator`, `TasksController::search`, `TaskCustomFieldIndexer`), not taken from a report. **(1) The re-run promise was false whenever the command carried `--options`.** `typeGates` calls `assertOptionsMatchTarget` **before** the `$from === $toType` no-op short-circuit, and that helper throws a plain `ValidationException` for same-type + non-null options (*"This request does not change the type, so it is an options edit — send it to PATCH …"*). So once a conversion has landed, re-running **the exact same command** with `--options` on it does not answer as a server-side no-op — it answers **422**, explained by an arm that names only what the server said. The two arms whose prescribed re-run can meet a conversion that ALREADY LANDED are exactly the two that cannot read their own outcome (`000` transport, and a `2xx` whose body nothing could be read out of) — the file's other re-run prescriptions (**412**, the zero-offender **422**, the offender-cap notice) each report a refusal that wrote nothing, and `--options` falsifies none of them — which is how the false advice reached the operator at the one moment they could not tell whether the write had landed. Fixed as **one owner** rather than at the two sites: `_kbc_field_conversion_rerun_note <have-options> <restamp-dl>` emits the whole sentence — the directive **and** its justification, since `--options` falsifies both — and `_kbc_unfinished_write_note` gives up the `Re-run this exact command:` half it used to hardcode (its callers now hand in the sentence; it keeps the UNKNOWN half — which is the conversion's `000` arm's, **not** every caller's: the restamp pass's failed-`PATCH` bucket is filled by `kb_api PATCH … || …`, and `kb_api` returns **1** for a completed non-2xx exactly as for a transport `000`, so a `PATCH` the server REFUSED — status already on stderr — is reported through this same *did not complete* wording; telling the two apart is a change at `kb_api`'s return contract and is not made here). `_kbc_field_change_type_report` reports a *response* and cannot see the request's flags, so the predicate is a seventh parameter rather than a re-derivation. Two hand-edited call sites were the shape that let this survive two fix rounds — one of them was corrected while the other kept the claim. **⚠ Wording and plumbing only: no rc moved at any site**, and `--restamp-dl`'s own re-run lines are untouched because they are unreachable with `--options` (the flag forces `--to string`, on which the server refuses options twice over — prohibited on a scalar target, and an options edit on a same-type request — both **422**, both before any `2xx`); that reachability is now stated at the function that relies on it instead of being re-derived. The plain-`ValidationException` arm's own three-item list of the server's option rules is **deleted** rather than extended to four: it was a client-side copy of a rule set the file's `--options` passthrough comment already forbids copying, it had drifted on the fourth rule, and the body echoed above it names the rule that actually fired. **(2) `field delete`'s fail-closed census is blind to archived and soft-deleted carriers.** `_kbc_field_populated` censuses through `GET /tasks/search.json`, which applies the default `SoftDeletes` scope **and** `whereNull('archived_at')`, while both behaviours the refusal exists for are **board-wide**: `CustomFieldMutator::delete` leaves `tasks.payload` untouched for every carrier and `TaskCustomFieldIndexer::purgeField` is `where board_id … where field_key … delete()` with no archive and no trash filter. A board whose only `dl_number` carriers are archived therefore prints *0 of 412*, does not refuse, prints no `--orphan-values` warning, purges the index for all of them and exits **0**; un-archiving one puts an undefined payload key back into the live set. **The disclosure is built at the census OWNER, on the line that prints the denominator, so both consumers inherit it** — the two previous MAJOR fixes on this card hoisted a note (`_kbc_field_census_scope_note`) and then wired it to `--restamp-dl` alone, leaving the other consumer of the same primitive stating the same unqualified number, and the new `_kbc_field_census_population_note` is the one owner of the population fact that both that note and the denominator line now embed. **⛔ The refusal is NOT widened.** Making `delete` refuse on carriers it cannot see changes what the tool rejects; that is ask-first and is recorded as a request on card#6525, not decided here — the gate's rc is byte-identical either side of this change, asserted as such, with the gate moved **both** ways as the control that those assertions can fail. The doc surfaces the two defects falsify are corrected in the same change: the `WHY delete IS FAIL-CLOSED` header block (which now says what "fail-closed" is quantified over), the `field delete` help text (*"an unpopulated delete is then distinguishable from a delete whose census never ran"* — a zero census establishes neither), README's `field delete` and `--options` bullets and its re-run sentence, and `_kbc_field_restamp_dl`'s header, which named one of its three parameters. **Mutation battery:** the **31** new assertions were driven by **9** named single-defect mutations, each restored from a byte snapshot, `cmp`'d, and the suite re-run clean — the options predicate ignored inside the shared owner (**8** red, both arms), the predicate not plumbed to the response reporter (**3** red, the `000` arm only, which is what proves the two arms are separately carried), the disclosure stripped from the denominator line (**6** red, across all three consumer legs), the fake's same-type-with-options refusal deleted (**3** red — the arm no invocation in the suite had ever reached, since every other `--options` leg targets a type the field is not), the rule enumeration restored (**2** red), the re-run directive dropped at the restamp site (**1** red), the re-run promise grafted back onto a **refusal** arm (**1** red — the shape a prior round of this card shipped), and the delete gate moved both ways (**12** and **5** red). **Eight** are named rather than counted as covered: the rc + arm-identity assertions on each options leg (**4**) are that branch's controls that the conditional changed nothing else and are held identically by the no-options legs; the two-run leg's run-1 preconditions (**2**) are what make run 2 a re-run of a *landed* conversion and are over-determined by the conversion legs above them; and one *not mis-reported as a pair refusal* and one restamp `rc 0` are asserted verbatim on pre-existing legs whose guards are covered there.

  **Fourth fix round — comments and docs only: four statements this commit's own prose makes about this file that the file does not honour.** Each was verified at source before it was touched; **no executable line, no printed message, no rc and no test assertion moved** (the field suite's **421** assertions and the 33-suite sweep's **3672** are identical either side, which is the check that nothing executable was edited). **(1) *the `000` arm makes the same claim*** — it does not. The unreadable-`2xx` arm feeds the shared re-run owner the run's own `restamp_dl`; the `000` arm feeds it a **hard-empty** restamp predicate, so with `--restamp-dl` the two arms print **different** sentences and only the `2xx` one names the pass. The file already said the true thing two hunks earlier (*the restamp pass is not named here on either branch*) and contradicted itself here. README's knock-on is fixed with it: the inserted *same sentence … `000` transport arm* claim had re-pointed the following sentence's antecedent — *what it says about the restamp pass depends on whether one was asked for* — at the `000` arm, where it is false. **(2) `_kbc_unfinished_write_note`'s UNKNOWN half is justified as the half *every caller shares*, and it is not shared.** `kb_api` returns **1** for a transport failure (`KB_HTTP=000`) and for **any** completed non-2xx alike, and the restamp pass's `patch_failed` bucket is filled by `kb_api PATCH … || patch_failed+=(…)`, so a 403/404/422/500 lands in it — and the run prints the server's status and then says the request *did not complete*. The header now states what that half **is** true of (the conversion's `000` arm) and where it does **not** hold; the printed wording is untouched, and so is `kb_api`'s return contract, which is the actual root cause and is not this round's to change. **(3) *on every run of both write verbs*** — `_kbc_field_populated` has exactly **two** call sites: `field delete` (every run that resolves its `--field`) and the `--restamp-dl` pass. A plain `retype --to string` takes **no census at all**, and a `--restamp-dl` run whose conversion does not answer a readable `2xx` returns before the pass ever runs. **(4) *`000` is the only outcome this function reports that prescribes a re-run*** — three others do: **412** (*re-read the definition … and re-run*), the zero-offender **422** (*re-run PLAIN, without the flag*) and the offender-cap notice (*fix the named ones and re-run*). Only the quantifier was wrong; the reasoning the `<have-options>` parameter rests on is kept and now stated as what actually distinguishes the arm — `000` is the only outcome whose re-run can meet the conversion **already landed as asked**, i.e. the same-type request `--options` turns into a 422, while the other three report a refusal that wrote nothing (412 additionally sends the operator back to `field list` rather than promising a no-op). **Also corrected:** the census-population note's *it is asked otherwise nowhere in this file* named the wrong scope — the census URL is built in `_kb-board-lib.sh`'s `fetch_board_cards`, not here; it sends `board_id`, `limit` and `page` and no archive or trash parameter, so the guarantee itself holds. **⛔ One residual, deliberately not fixed here:** the plain-`ValidationException` 422 arm prints *every rule about `--options` is the server's, and they all answer here*, which contradicts this file's own documented exception — the **duplicate-value** refusal is this tool's own, at **rc 2**, before the wire. It is unreachable-false (a duplicate never reaches that arm), and correcting it means editing **printed message text**, which this round is scoped out of.

  **Fifth fix round — the `000` arm's re-run line stops being a directive at all.** Three rounds each corrected this one sentence and each shipped a claim that is false on one branch, because on the transport-failure arm there is no true single directive to pick. `typeGates` calls `assertOptionsMatchTarget` **before** the `$from === $toType` short-circuit, so with `--options` on the command a conversion that **landed** refuses the identical re-run as an options edit (422), while a conversion that did **not** land is resolved by exactly that re-run — and dropping the options from a conversion still to happen is not neutral either (converting into an option type from a non-option source without them is a 422; between the two option types it lands carrying the **stored** option set, which is not the set that was asked for). The `000` arm is *defined* by not knowing which of those two boards it is on — that is its entire reason to exist — so every unconditional directive it can print is right only by predicting the branch. **So the SHAPE changed rather than the wording.** With `--options` and no landed-ness, the arm now prescribes the **definition read first** — `field list`, the same resolution the **412** arm has always prescribed, reused rather than a second shape invented — and branches on what the read shows, naming the request's own types: reading the **target** type means the conversion **LANDED**, so re-run **without** `--options`; still reading the **source** type means it did **not**, so re-run **the exact command**. That first step is correct whichever way the write went, so the sentence cannot be false on either branch — true by construction rather than by guessing right, which is the property the three previous versions lacked. **The unreadable-`2xx` arm is ruled on explicitly and does NOT get this shape:** a `2xx` **is** the server's landed-ness guarantee (it does not answer `2xx` for a refusal), so that arm already knows what the read would say; prescribing it there would offer to resolve a settled question, and the second branch — *if it still reads `<from>`, it did not land* — names a state that arm's own `2xx` excludes. Its unconditional *drop the options* directive is kept unchanged. Both arms stay in the **one owner**, now `_kbc_field_conversion_rerun_note <have-options> <restamp-dl> <landed-known> <from> <to>`, every predicate plumbed from the call site for the reason `<have-options>` already was — two sites spelling this sentence separately is how the `000` arm came to carry a directive its sibling had already had corrected — and the type names come from the request because on a `000` there is no response to derive them from. **⚠ Wording and plumbing only: no rc moved, no control flow beyond the branch text, and the no-`--options` sentence and both `--restamp-dl` arms are untouched** (re-running the exact command is safe there whether or not it landed, so that branch was never conditional on anything). **Mutation battery:** the **13** new assertions were driven by **8** named single-defect mutations, each restored from a byte snapshot, `cmp`'d, and the suite re-run clean — the owner ignoring `<landed-known>` (**3** red, including the `2xx` arm's own directive), the read-then-branch shape reverted to the previous round's unconditional one (**7**), `<landed-known>` not plumbed at the `000` site (**7**, every one of them on that arm and none on the `2xx` one, which is what proves the two arms are separately carried), `<have-options>` not plumbed there either — the shape round 2b shipped (**9**), the two branch type names swapped (**4**, across both type pairs) and then **hardcoded** to one pair (**3**, all on the second pair's leg), the types not plumbed at all (**6**), and the read named **last** instead of first (**1** — the assertion that pins the read as the first thing the sentence says, which is what makes the directives conditional on something the operator has established). **One** is named rather than counted as covered: the second type pair's `rc 1` is that leg's own control that the branch wording changed nothing else, and is over-determined (the `000` arm returns 1 by construction of the route).

  **Testing.** `tests/kbcard-field-selftest.sh` runs **432** assertions over a stateful board fake whose DELETE arm deliberately does not cascade into the card payloads — that IS behaviour (A), and a fake that cascaded it would let `delete`'s refusal be deleted with every assertion still green. The conversion route is **modelled, not canned**: the option rules, the pair matrix (`boolean` is terminal in both directions), the compare-and-swap, the no-op short-circuit and the offender scan are transcribed from `CustomFieldMutator`, so a client-side re-derivation creeping back in reds against the **server's** rule rather than against a fixture written to match the client; only the outcomes a fake board cannot produce are canned bodies (the two 413 rails, a concurrent 404, a `000` transport failure, a server-capped offender list), and the archived/soft-deleted carriers the conversion also converts are DECLARED, since a fake board cannot hold a card its own fetch never returns. **53 mutations** put **144 of the 150** assertions of the rewrite's own section on the record as **watched red**, each restored from a byte snapshot with the suite re-run clean (an inverse string swap is not a restore). The six that no mutation could red are named rather than counted as covered: five are rc-2 argument-guard assertions that are **over-determined** — removing the guard still refuses at rc 2 by a second path, measured — so they pin the verb's rc contract and not the guard they sit next to, and the sixth (`--field` enumeration) is owned by the shared resolver and asserted identically in the `set-options` section. The **41** assertions added by the fix round above were watched red the same way: **23** red against the whole pre-fix `bin/kbcard` (the two MAJORs and three of the MINORs), and the rest under **10** named single-defect mutations — a silenced 413 arm, a report that drops the server's body, a non-2xx path returning 0, the truncation disclosure made a refusal, an acknowledgement built from the *shown* offenders instead of `ref_impact_task_ids`, a remainder that refuses instead of canonicalizing the visible cards, an off-by-one in the population compare, an unconditional truncation notice, an out-of-census arm that starts writing, and the ack-shift fixture restored to the `offender_count: 0` body the server does not send. **Three** are named rather than counted as covered: two *nothing was written* board-state controls on refusal legs (over-determined — a refusal writes nothing by construction of the route) and one renamed pre-existing assertion. The **97** assertions added by the second fix round were watched red under **22** named single-defect mutations, each restored from a byte snapshot and `cmp`'d: the census scope dropped from the completeness line and from the empty-census line (separately), the empty-census line reverted to its *no card carries* wording, the scanned signal re-inferred from `converted_task_count == 0` and then hardcoded on, the offender-cap tail reverted and then branch-inverted, the duplicate refusal deleted and then stripped of the clause naming its owner, that same clause ADDED to the empty-value refusal (whose rule is the server's — a check that only ever fires on a missing string cannot tell a mis-attributed one), the label disclosure silenced / made unconditional / moved to pre-flight / made to re-read the field index, the two unverified-delete messages swapped, a refused create POST turned into a success, and — in the fake — the live-growth knob stopped appending. The re-run scoping adds five: both restamp arms reverted to their *finishes the rest* wording, the non-DL carve-out made UNCONDITIONAL and then deleted outright, `notdl` counted INTO the finishable set (the arithmetic error the carve-out exists to prevent), and the unreadable-echo arm's *finishes any `--restamp-dl` pass* restored. The conditional-restamp correction adds **two** more over the **10** assertions of the new no-flag leg, and the two branches now discriminate each other: making the flagged wording unconditional again reds the **3** new assertions that pin the restamp claim's ABSENCE (the skipped-pass sentence, the word *restamp* at all, and the non-DL-value clause), while making the plain wording unconditional reds **2** pre-existing assertions on the flag-present leg — each mutation restored from a byte snapshot and `cmp`'d, with the suite re-run clean. The other **7** of the new leg are named rather than counted as covered: they re-pin the arm's rc 1, its UNCONFIRMED wording, its empty stdout, the safe-re-run and server-side-no-op halves of the sentence, and that it issues no `PATCH` and no board read — all of which the flag-present leg already holds, and which are here as this branch's own controls that the conditional changed nothing else. **One assertion is named rather than counted as covered:** the two-run leg's *run 2 writes no card* is a delta against run 1's own count and is over-determined (a no-op re-run over canonical values writes nothing by two independent paths). ⚠ **The one thing the fake still cannot express is an archived carrier on the no-op path**, because the server cannot either — `noop()` hardcodes `convertedTaskCount: 0` before any scan — so that leg is driven as the **two-run sequence** the tool's own remediation prescribes rather than by letting the out-of-census knob leak into an arm the server never consults it from. Bending the fake there would have made every assertion built on it a measurement of the fixture.

- **card#6426** — **a `2xx` whose body these tools cannot read no longer kills them at `jq`'s exit 5: every single-card / single-field read and every write echo now refuses one in its own tool's words, at that tool's own documented rc.** ⚠ **Consumers who vendor: re-vendor `_kb-board-lib.sh` TOGETHER with `bin/kbcard`, `bin/adopt-to-dl`, `bin/dl-a1-register-field` and `bin/next-dl`** — the tolerant parse those bins now call lives in the lib, so a new bin beside an old lib fails at runtime with `kb_parse_resp: command not found`. (The lib has always been a co-vendoring requirement — `ADOPTION.md`, `docs/INSTALL.md` §6b, and the drift check's `MISSING-LIB` probe — this release is the first where a stale copy breaks a working command rather than a new one.)

  **The state.** `kb_api` decides success on the **HTTP status class alone**, so a `2xx` carrying a proxy's HTML error page, a captive-portal interstitial or a truncated read arrives at every projection as a success. An unguarded `jq` there exits **5** under `set -e`, with jq's raw parse error as the caller's whole diagnostic — a status none of these tools documents, from a program the caller never ran. card#6051 closed that for three `kbcard` sites with a file-local helper; this change **hoists the helper into `_kb-board-lib.sh` as `kb_parse_resp`** (mechanism in the primitive, refusal policy at each caller — `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` ground rule 5) and adopts it at every `kb_api` / `kb_api_status` projection in the bins listed above.

  **⚠ THE WHOLE-BOARD READ IS NOT COVERED, and the boundary is stated rather than implied.** `fetch_board_cards` — the paginator behind `kbcard list`, `kbcard archive`'s twin check, `next-dl`'s fallback board-max scan, `dl-a0-backfill-triaged`, `board-card-start`, `board-snapshot` and `board-stats` — still parses each page with its own inline `jq … 2>/dev/null`, so a `2xx` whose body is not JSON at all is answered with **`[]` at rc 0 and nothing on stderr** (measured: `kbcard list` against a `200` carrying `<html>502</html>`). That is not a refusal, and it is exactly the plausible-looking value this change closes elsewhere: indistinguishable from a board with no cards. Only the CLAIM was this card's to correct; the behaviour is **card#6594**, which owns it — and which lands in **this same release**, so the boundary stated here is closed by the entry below. That entry is the owner of the paginator's behaviour; this paragraph records only what *this* change did and did not reach. What a `[]` from `list` does and does not establish is stated in one place only — README § Reliability posture — and is deliberately not restated here.

  **What a caller sees change:** `create-card`, `move`, `patch`, `link` and `field set-options` report an unreadable write **echo** as an UNCONFIRMED write at **rc 1** (was rc 5); `field list` and the `--field` resolution report an unreadable field read at **rc 1**, never as "this board has no custom fields"; an external-id (`ID-OR-EXT`) lookup whose result list cannot be read says so instead of answering **"no task found"**, which is a claim about the board a read that never reached one cannot make; `archive`'s safety gate already failed closed and now does it without printing jq's parse error over its own refusal; `adopt-to-dl` refuses rather than feeding an unreadable body to the guard that keeps an adoption on the **right board**; `dl-a1-register-field` completes its run (and its throwaway teardown — a leaked sentinel card poisons the DL minter) instead of dying between the create and the cleanup.

  **`patch --tags/--type/--triaged` refuses at rc 2 and issues no PATCH at all** when the tag read it must merge into yields no usable tag list — that list is replaced wholesale by the API, so writing one built from nothing would have destroyed the card's tags. **Three body shapes reach that harm without jq ever faulting, so a tolerant parse alone does not close it, and the read is shape-tested before the wire.** (1) A body that PARSES and simply carries no card: on `{"ok":true}` or `{"data":null}`, `.data.tags // []` supplies `[]` — non-empty — so the wipe went *through* the existing guard (measured: rc 0, a PATCH body of `{"tags":["triaged"]}`, every existing tag gone). `.data` is therefore required to be an **object**.
