A board is never silently omitted, including when the aggregation itself fails on its data. Read-only (every call is a `GET`); rc 0 = the report ran, including a partial one, rc 1 = no board could be read at all so there is nothing to believe, rc 2 = usage. `--format json` carries exactly what the text prints because both are rendered from **one** derivation. **Added to the shared lib:** `kb_board_roster` — the roster-file parser hoisted for its second caller, with discovery of every `~/.kanban-*-board.env` as its fallback. `board-snapshot` deliberately still carries the inline original: its fallback is a different one (two hardcoded board names, kept for back-compat), so adopting the primitive there changes what a roster-less box renders at SessionStart and is a decision rather than a cleanup — recorded at the function, not left implicit. **Coverage:** new `tests/board-stats-selftest.sh` (network-free — the argument surface including every spec `date(1)` would have answered at rc 0, the pagination cutoff against a fixture whose third page holds rows a correct run must never reach, the log-exhausted-inside-the-window case with the control that flips it back, the `lane_type` derivation with the control that strips the lane types and watches the class empty, the swimlane-only move that must not become a transition, the human/service split in both directions plus the API's third `actor_type` value, and the text renderer's wash/partial/truncation rows), each leg watched red under a mutation of the code it guards — the assertion count is the suite's own output (`bash tests/board-stats-selftest.sh | grep -c '^  ok'`), re-derivable rather than recorded here; `tests/kb-board-lib-selftest.sh` gains the `kb_board_roster` cases. **One existing check changed:** `help-output-selftest.sh`'s "does not leak code past the header" leg matched the literal `set -euo pipefail`, which is not the first post-header line in a fail-soft bin — it now **derives** that canary from the file under test and witnesses it present in the file first, so the absence assertion cannot pass vacuously. **Consumer impact:** additive. `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` are untouched, no existing CLI's behaviour or exit codes change, and `_kb-board-lib.sh` gains one new function and no altered one — but the lib IS co-vendored, and `board-stats` is a **lib-sourcing** bin, so a vendor-by-copy consumer that takes it must copy `_kb-board-lib.sh` beside it (ADOPTION.md and `docs/INSTALL.md` §6b list it accordingly). A `PATH` install must re-run the symlink loop for the new bin, which `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check` now names in its `TOOLS` set.

- **card#5779** — **`kbcard list`'s projected field set is the only surface a consumer can filter on, and it dropped the two fields cards are actually hunted by.** The projection emitted `id`, `name`, `stage`, `type`, `swimlane_id`, `swimlane`, `dl`, `pr` — so a downstream `grep`/`jq select` over `tags` or `external_id` matched nothing on every card, and an empty result is indistinguishable from an empty board. A live consumer read it as **"745 cards, 0 dependabot cards"** while the `id:dep:…` provenance tag was on the card the whole time and `kbcard show` returned it. `tags` and `external_id` now ride every row (absent tags project as `[]` — the same normalization the `--type` tag filter already applies, so the filter and the emitted value cannot disagree; absent `external_id` projects `null`, like `dl`/`pr`). `description` stays deliberately **out**: it is unbounded free text on every row of a whole-board read, and `show` is where a card is read whole. **Additive — no key changed or was removed**, so a `jq`/key-access consumer is unaffected. **The tool's own flag surface was already honest** and is unchanged: `list` takes only `--column`/`--type`/`--swimlane`, all three filter on projected fields, and any other argument already exited 2 — the silent-0 lived entirely in downstream filtering, which is why the fix widens the projection rather than adding a refusal for a state that cannot occur. **Also: a filtered read now names its denominator on stderr** — one line, `kbcard: list <flags as typed>: <M> of <N> board cards matched`, so a suspicious `0`-of-`N` is legible at the call site. **stdout is byte-identical** (still just the array, trailing newline included — asserted by `cmp`), so nothing parsing it is affected; a consumer that folds stderr into stdout (`2>&1`) will see the line. An **unfiltered** read prints nothing: the array's own length already IS the denominator. `--tag` was considered and **not** added — the query that motivated this is a *prefix* over the tag namespace (`id:dep:`), which an exact-match `--tag` cannot answer, so it would ship a flag reading as "the tag filter" that returns 0 for the very case at hand; with `tags` now projected, a `jq select`/`grep` answers both prefix and exact.

## [0.27.0] - 2026-08-11

**Minor — a new host-local instrument that asks whether the fix a CLOSED Dependabot alert claims is actually present in what is deployed and running, wired as a fourth advisory leg of `board-session-close`.** 2 PRs since v0.26.0 (#230–#231): one feature, plus a docs-only solo-orientation sync (coord v0.33.0, #230). **Minor** per `VERSIONING.md § Bump sizing` — a new `bin/` tool plus new user-visible output on an existing one.

**⚠ UPGRADING — re-run the `PATH` symlink loop (`VERSIONING.md` step 11).** This release ADDS a bin, and a symlink tracks its target's *content*, not the *set* of tools: without the loop `dependabot-deploy-reconcile` never lands on `PATH`, and `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check` — which gains it in its `TOOLS` set — prints a new warn line naming exactly that. The warn line is the check doing its job; the fix is the re-symlink it already prescribes. **Two new preflight entries in `docs/INSTALL.md` §0, neither fatal:** `ps` (absent ⇒ the new tool reports an instrument failure rather than a wrong answer) and coreutils `timeout` (absent — the default on macOS, and possible under the MSYS install — ⇒ `board-session-close`'s advisory legs still run, each printing a one-line warning that it is running **unbounded**).

**No narrowings this release, and no interface change for action-pinning consumers** — `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` are untouched, as are the board-writing CLIs (`bin/kbcard`, `bin/board-card-start`, `bin/next-dl`, `bin/promote-released-cards`, `bin/release-pr-body`). Two existing bins do change what a consumer sees: **`board-session-close`** grows a `── Dependabot fixed-alert vs deployed-tree reconciliation ──` section with its own warnings, runs **both** advisory legs under a 60s wall-clock bound (a leg killed at the budget prints a ⚠ and still never blocks the close), and reworded the archive leg's missing-sibling warning slightly — which matters only to anything matching on its exact text; **`agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`** gains one `TOOLS` entry. New files: `bin/dependabot-deploy-reconcile` + `bin/_dependabot-reconcile.py` (host-local by design — fixed scope, not a vendorable per-repo tool).

### Added

- **card#6277** — **`bin/dependabot-deploy-reconcile`: does the fix a CLOSED Dependabot alert claims actually exist in what is deployed and RUNNING?** An alert closes when its fix reaches the repository's default branch. Production serves something else — a tag, a release clone, an installed dependency tree — cut by a separate process on its own cadence, and nothing in the toolkit compared the two. A repository whose alert list reads 0-open can therefore be serving the vulnerable version indefinitely, with no symptom anywhere and no artifact recording that nobody checked. The new tool implements **one leg**: an installed npm dependency tree, whose evidence class is `installed-file` — the identity IS the version of the file the runtime loads, so no git tag is read and none is claimed. It resolves the deployed directory from the MCP config that **launches** the server (never a hardcoded path — that resolution is what ties "the tree I read" and "the tree it is running" to the same claim), reads each alerted package's installed `package.json` version, and compares it against the alert's own `first_patched_version` under **SemVer 2.0 precedence**. **The comparator is an explicit conservative proxy — `installed >= first_patched_version`, and `vulnerable_version_range` is never parsed:** each ecosystem's range grammar differs with no single published spec, and a range-parsing bug is a silent FALSE NEGATIVE (a genuinely vulnerable version cleared), where the proxy can only ever over-report. `>=` and not `>`, so a version EQUAL to the first patched version is FIXED — a boundary the suite exercises through the shipped argument path in both directions (equal ⇒ `FIXED_CONFIRMED`, one patch below ⇒ `STILL_EXPOSED`), because a comparator accidentally spelled `>` passes every healthy-tree case. **Four dispositions, and only two of them are answers:** `FIXED_CONFIRMED` / `STILL_EXPOSED` / `UNDECIDABLE` (the read is fine, the comparison cannot be made — no `first_patched_version`, a non-semver version, the package is not in the deployment) / `UNVERIFIABLE` (the read itself cannot be trusted — no running process for this install, a `package.json` newer than the running process, a nested `node_modules` copy making the loaded copy ambiguous, an alert outside the leg's declared ecosystem/manifest set). Nothing is ever silently skipped: an alert this leg does not cover gets a row naming why. **The tree's own readability is gated before any per-package read, and that gate is the difference between a finding and a fiction:** with `node_modules` absent or unreadable, every alerted package's `package.json` is missing, and answered one package at a time that renders as `UNDECIDABLE: package not in deployment` — a decidable FACT about the deployment minted out of a read that never happened, at rc 0, beside a coverage entry still claiming the install is covered. The window is the ordinary one (`npm ci` REMOVES `node_modules` before repopulating it, and this runs at session close), so every alert now resolves `UNVERIFIABLE: node_modules not readable at <path>` and the coverage entry flips to `{covered: false}` carrying the **same reason string** — rows and coverage statement are one claim about one install, and an instrument reporting both ways leaves a reader no way to tell which half to believe. Absent and unreadable are named apart because the operator remedies differ (wait out the reinstall vs. fix the permissions). **That predicate is now one primitive applied at every directory read rather than a check written per site** — a sibling audit found the same shape open at two more of them, and both mattered: the nested-copy scan (`os.walk` swallows every listing error by default, and a subtree it cannot enter reads as "no nested copy here", which is precisely what LETS the top-level read stand — so an unreadable subtree did not degrade the verdict, it UPGRADED it to `FIXED_CONFIRMED`), and the per-package read (`os.path.exists` answers False for a package that is missing AND for one whose directory cannot be traversed, so the code contradicted its own docstring's claim to separate the two). The readability test is read+execute, not read: a directory with read but no execute lists its children and then reports every one of them as a non-directory, which is the quietest form of the same lie. The nested-copy scan now classifies **every** directory entry positively — a plain directory, a plain file, or a symlink resolving to a regular file are the only outcomes confirmed harmless, and everything else is recorded — because enumerating the unsafe shapes kept missing one: a symlinked subtree escaped the walk entirely, and then a symlink whose *target* could not be stat'd escaped the symlink check too (`os.walk` swallows the error and files it as a regular file). A symlinked subtree is recorded rather than followed — under the default `followlinks=False` a symlinked directory is listed but never walked, so neither the error handler nor the per-directory check ever sees it, and an unwalked subtree reads as "no nested copy here" (file-symlinks, as in `node_modules/.bin`, correctly do not fire it). **No count of these routes is claimed in prose any more** — that claim has been falsified twice — so the code points at the fixture set that asserts them instead. **Every alert query is gated, because this API fails in ways a status code does not show** — measured, not assumed: an **unknown** state value answers **200 with `[]`** (`?state=all` and `?state=bogus` both do), so a typo'd filter yields an empty population at a success status, indistinguishable from a real "no alerts in this state". A **mis-cased known** value is not that case — the filter is applied case-insensitively (`?state=Fixed` returns the same set as `?state=fixed`) — so it is the enum guard, not casing luck, that keeps a typo out; the per-response `.state` re-assert is a **boundary check on a remote answer**, kept because no gate should rest on the absence of a currently-known bad shape. So each query checks the command's rc, asserts the body is a JSON array, sends only a literal from a validated enum, and **re-asserts every returned alert's own `.state`** against the one queried; the three excluded states are **counted** and reported (so `state=fixed` is a named inclusion with measured exclusions rather than a silent filter) and the integrity control sums **all four** per-state counts — the included state as well as the three excluded — against an unfiltered query, so a short read of the population itself is caught and not only a short read of what was excluded, with a zero-length population treated as an **instrument failure (rc 1)** — not as an `UNVERIFIABLE` disposition, which is a per-alert verdict and cannot be emitted over zero rows — unless that unfiltered query confirms the repository genuinely has zero alerts in every state. **The staleness input is each READ `package.json`'s mtime against the running process's start time**, not the `node_modules` directory's: a reinstall that rewrites one package leaves the directory mtime saying nothing about the file the process actually loaded, and this tool runs at session close on a host where the tree may have just been reinstalled. Process detection reads `ps` whole and filters in-process — `pgrep -f`/`pkill -f` are refused rather than escaped, since their pattern is matched against the very argv that carries it. Installs on the host **other** than the one read are enumerated and reported `{covered: false}` with the reason, so the run states its own coverage instead of implying it — and that detection is keyed on the `channel-servers/<file>.mjs` path shape alone rather than on the repository name, because an install cloned into a differently-named directory is still one this run does not cover, and a coverage statement that silently omits an install reads as a complete one. **Fixed scope, host-local by design:** alerts are queried for one named repository, and the deployed directory is resolved from this host's own MCP server entry — it is the agent host's session instrument, not a vendorable per-repo tool. Output is one line per alert plus a **scope object** (run identity, states queried with their counts, per-install coverage, denominator, any injected identity) emitted into **both** stdout and a JSON artifact under `~/.cache/coord/dependabot-reconcile/`. **Exit contract, and it is the point of the tool being advisory:** rc 0 = the instrument ran and every alert carries a disposition, INCLUDING `STILL_EXPOSED` and `UNVERIFIABLE` rows, which are findings reported in the output and deliberately not folded into the exit code; rc 1 = an input was unreadable, a gate failed, **or the evidence artifact could not be written** — so the dispositions are absent, untrustworthy, or unrecorded (where a gate is what failed, the artifact still records what was measured); rc 2 = usage error. The 1-vs-2 split follows `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`, the closest sibling. **That three-value contract is measured on the path `board-session-close` takes, and is not yet universal on a hand-run one:** the advisory leg captures the tool's output, so its stdout is never closed early, but a **manual** invocation piped into a reader that exits first escapes as a `BrokenPipeError` at **rc 120** — the top-level backstop wraps the measurement phase and not the output phase. Known and tracked at release, bounded to piped manual invocation; the session-close leg is unaffected (verified rc 0 on that path). **A host that is not configured for this tool is not a broken one:** with no `~/.mcp.json`, or one declaring no `mcpServers.kanbanboard-agent` entry, the run prints a single not-configured line, writes **no artifact**, and exits **0** — previously this was a rendered INSTRUMENT FAILURE at rc 1 at every session close, forever, on every host that was never in scope, and a warning that always fires is one nobody reads on the day it means something. A declared entry that launches no `.mjs` is the opposite case — something is meant to run here and cannot be located — and stays rc 1, as does a present-but-unparseable config. **A package name is validated POSITIVELY — accepted only in npm's two legal shapes — and a bad one costs only its own alert.** Every earlier version of this check enumerated BAD shapes, and each review round found one the list had not named; the last two were the expensive kind. A `None` name (through an `or ""`) path-joined to the `node_modules` ROOT and reported `FIXED_CONFIRMED` about a package that does not exist, and a `../`-bearing name read a file OUTSIDE the deployed tree and minted `FIXED_CONFIRMED` from it. The rule is now what a name IS — one path component, or `@scope/name`, with no empty, relative, separator- or NUL-bearing segment — and everything else is refused. It is checked only for alerts this leg actually handles, since the grammar is npm's and another ecosystem's names are legal in their own (composer's `vendor/package` is the live case). Backing it, independent of it: a containment invariant asserting every path this leg reads resolves strictly under the deployed tree — the check that holds when a grammar turns out to be wrong about a shape, which is the thing that has actually kept happening. A malformed name now yields an `UNVERIFIABLE` row naming the reason at rc 0, rather than failing the run: one bad alert must not destroy the other eighteen measurements. **An unexpected exception anywhere in the run now still produces an artifact.** A top-level backstop turns anything unanticipated into an instrument failure in the tool's own vocabulary — rc 1, `INSTRUMENT FAILURE` framing, the rows and scope measured up to the fault, and the traceback recorded under `scope.unhandled_traceback` — instead of a raw traceback with no artifact at all, which made the exit contract's promise that a failed gate still records what was measured false exactly when it mattered. Three wrong-typed scalars in an alert payload were escaping that way (a `number` mixing int and str so the population could not be sorted, an unhashable package name, a non-string name reaching a path join); each also gets a NAMED cause in the query gate, because the backstop can only say "unhandled TypeError" and a named cause is what makes a report actionable. **Malformed API and file payloads now degrade to dispositions instead of tracebacks.** An alerts array whose ELEMENTS are not objects passed the array check and then killed the first field read — an `AttributeError`, no `INSTRUMENT FAILURE` framing and no artifact, contradicting the exit contract's promise that a gate failure still records what was measured; the element shape is asserted in the query gate so no consumer repeats the check. The same assumption one level down (`dependency`, `dependency.package`, and a `package.json` parsing to a JSON array) was closed with it: the `x.get(k) or {}` idiom guards against null and a missing key, but a present-but-wrong-typed value is truthy and sails through, so all three crashed. They now resolve to a named `UNVERIFIABLE` row, which is safe only because every field read out of those objects feeds a fail-closed disposition. A `--test-mode` flag routes artifacts to a **separate** directory so a synthetic run is never mixed into the evidence trail, and `--inject-identity` (accepted **only** alongside it — a stray one is a hard error, never a silent override) drives the disposition engine through the real argument path rather than a side harness. Wired as a fourth advisory leg of `board-session-close` on the same contract as the archive leg: a non-zero exit warns and never blocks the close. **New `tests/dependabot-reconcile-selftest.sh`** — network-free (`gh` and `ps` are stubbed from shellchecked `tests/_dependabot-*-stub.sh` files), each check pointed at a fixture that drives its failing branch with a control differing in one input — with one stated exception, below. **The figures are the suite's own measured output, re-derivable rather than recorded:** `bash tests/dependabot-reconcile-selftest.sh | grep -c '^  ok'` reports the assertion count (257 at this writing), and the suite's exit status is the only pass claim it makes. **The warranty is deliberately weaker than "every check can fail":** an adversarial re-read of this change mutated the shipped code and found two checks that could not — the excluded-state counts (every fixture held 0 in all three excluded states, so hardcoding the emission to 0 survived) and `gh_alerts`' call to the `state=` validator (for the only literals a real run passes, the validated and unvalidated URLs are byte-identical). Both are now pinned — the first by a fixture carrying **distinct non-zero** counts in all three excluded states plus an all-zero control over the same keys, the second by a unit probe that drives `gh_alerts` with `subprocess` replaced by a recorder — and each was watched red under the mutation it closes and green on restore. **A third gap is stated rather than papered over:** three arms of the nested-copy classifier — the “neither a regular file nor a directory” fault append, and the two `OSError` handlers recording an entry that could not be classified or a directory that could not be listed — carry no fixture that reds them, so the per-check warranty above does not extend to them. The behaviour behind them was exercised by hand (a FIFO, a unix socket, a symlink to a character device, and an `ELOOP` chain), and each recorded a fault as designed; what is missing is the assertion, not the behaviour. Tracked for a follow-up fixture. `board-session-close`'s own suite gains the new leg's rc/indent/absent-sibling contract. **The advisory-leg shape is extracted rather than duplicated (canon #5, at the second caller):** `board-session-close` now delegates both its archive-eligible and its Dependabot legs through one `_bsc_advisory_leg` helper — sibling resolution beside the bin, an existence check whose predicate stays per-leg (one sibling ships mode 644 and is run through `python3`, so requiring `-x` would report the correctly-installed file as missing), the `2>&1` fold that keeps a delegate's stderr from reading as a ritual-level warning, the two-space indent, and a non-zero exit that warns without propagating. The per-leg wording stays distinguishable, and with both siblings present-and-quiet in each fixture the suite's absence assertions are back to their broad form: a healthy run must leave **every** leg silent about a missing sibling, not just the one a needle happened to name. **Consumer impact — the composite actions are untouched; two bins do change.** `promote/action.yml` and `release-artifacts/action.yml` are unchanged, so action-pinning consumers see no interface change, and the new tool is itself a host-local session instrument rather than a vendorable one. But "no existing `bin/` behaviour changes" would be false, so it is not claimed: **`board-session-close`** grows a new `── Dependabot fixed-alert vs deployed-tree reconciliation ──` section with its own warnings, and both its advisory legs now run under a **60s wall-clock bound** (a leg killed at the budget prints a ⚠ and still does not block the close) — and where coreutils `timeout` is absent, as on a default macOS or MSYS host, each leg still RUNS and prints a one-line warning that it is unbounded, because that bound was added to a helper extracted from two legs, one of which pre-dates it: making it a hard dependency would have broken a working leg to add a guarantee those hosts cannot have. `docs/INSTALL.md` §0 gains `timeout` and `ps` to its preflight — the archive leg's missing-sibling warning is also reworded slightly by that extraction, which matters only to anything matching on its exact text. **`agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`** gains one `TOOLS` entry, so an install that took the upgrade **without** re-symlinking `bin/` onto `PATH` gets a new warn line naming the missing tool — that line is the check doing its job, and the fix is the re-symlink it already prescribes.

## [0.26.0] - 2026-08-10

**Minor — `next-dl --peek` stops handing back an already-allocated DL number, and a repo whose commit subjects say `card#NNNN` can finally have its release-PR card-coverage gate fire.** 5 PRs since v0.25.0 (#224–#228). Minor per `VERSIONING.md § Bump sizing` — new user-visible surface (`promote-released-cards --cards`, the optional `.release-pr.json` `card_token_regex` key) alongside the fixes.

**⚠ UPGRADE FIRST IF ANYONE USES `next-dl --peek` TO CHOOSE A DECISION-LOG NUMBER.** Before this release `--peek` answered from an offline scan of the DL numbers that had reached a *card*, so any number already **claimed** from the board's DL counter but never stamped onto a card was invisible to it: `--peek` handed back a number the server had already issued, once per burned claim. A DL is a correlation key, so a reused one silently merges two unrelated work items, and the offline scan is structurally unable to detect that state — it is a floor, not the truth. `--peek` now reads the board's non-consuming DL-sequence counter and falls back to the offline scan only where that endpoint is absent, so a board whose kanban predates the inspect route sees no change and a board that has it starts getting the authoritative answer. If your `--peek` and a direct counter read disagree, your `next-dl` predates this fix. The default (claim) path is untouched.

**No narrowings this release.** Changed vendorable files: `bin/next-dl`, `bin/promote-released-cards`, `bin/release-pr-body`, plus a description-only correction to `release-artifacts/action.yml`; `promote/action.yml` and the board-writing CLIs (`bin/kbcard`, `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`) are unchanged.

### Added

- **card#5877** — **`.release-pr.json` gains `card_token_regex`, and `promote-released-cards` gains an opt-in `--cards`, so a repo whose commit subjects say `card#NNNN` can have its release-PR card-coverage gate actually fire.** `release-pr-body`'s `## Card coverage` section derived its manifest from `ref_token_regex` alone and short-circuited an empty one with a confident `_No shipped DL refs in range._`. In a repo whose subjects have moved to `card#NNNN` while that key still says `DL-`, the manifest is unconditionally empty, so the section reported clean **without checking anything** — a check that cannot fail. Reproduced before being fixed: over a range carrying a `card#` ref, with a board token present and a promoter that reports every ref it is handed as cardless, the section still rendered the clean line; the same range under a matching regex rendered the warning, so the reproduction discriminates. **The one-line config edit is a worse bug, and this is measured, not argued:** `promote-released-cards` reads the *same* key and matches the token's numeric part against `payload.dl_number`, so under a `card#[0-9]+` spelling a range naming `card#42` correlated with the card carrying **DL-42** — it moved that card, left card 42 alone, and summarised `1 moved, 0 no-card`. The two spellings are therefore two **id spaces** with two keys: `ref_token_regex`'s numeric part is a decision-log number matched against `payload.dl_number`; `card_token_regex`'s is a **card id** matched against the card's own `id`. **Consumer impact — additive, and nothing changes for a repo that adopts neither key.** `card_token_regex` is optional, and an existing consumer that does not add the key sees no change at all; note that `examples/release-pr.json.example` **does** set it, so a consumer copying that template starts with the coverage section enabled; `promote-released-cards --cards "12,34"` is opt-in and, with the flag absent, produces byte-identical stdout, stderr and PATCH set (diffed against the pre-change script across five invocation shapes: multi-DL with a cardless ref, the stage guard, an already-released card, a no-match set, and `--dry-run`). Two visible changes for a repo that **does** set the new key: the coverage section's prose now says "refs" rather than "DLs" (`_No shipped refs in range._`, `_All shipped refs have a tracking card._`), and the body gains a second footer `<!-- release-manifest:shipped-cards=5877,5874 -->` alongside the unchanged `shipped-refs` one — **bare ids, not tokens**, since the existing `tr '[:lower:]' '[:upper:]'` fold would otherwise deliver `CARD#5877` to a consumer. The bundled list also renders a card token where a commit has no DL token, DL-first so an existing repo's rendering is untouched. **Card ids are deliberately never derived from commit subjects on the MOVE path:** a descriptive citation ("supersedes card#1234") is common, and where a DL token's misfire is bounded by the card also needing a matching `dl_number` stamp, an id *is* the match — so a passing mention would relocate an unrelated card. `--cards` takes them explicitly and is strictly numeric (`--cards "card#12"` is a config error, not a lenient parse); only `release-pr-body`'s read-only coverage call derives them, under `--dry-run`, where the worst outcome is a line reading "covered". Both selftests were **watched red on the pre-change tree** — the coverage assertions fail and the run dies at the unknown `--card-manifest` — each with a control that flips only the board's contents, so "reports every ref" cannot satisfy them. Three hazards found by the reread of this change's own diff are closed with it. A `set -o pipefail` one in `bundle_lines`: a subject with no token makes its `grep` exit 1 and the assignment inherit it, and only bash's errexit suppression for a command substitution inside an assignment kept the bundled list from truncating at the first token-less commit — the no-match case is now explicitly valid rather than valid by where the caller happens to put the call. The card id is the matched token's **trailing** digit run rather than a `grep -oE '[0-9]+'` over the whole stream, because `card_token_regex` is operator-supplied and need not be `card#…`: a prefix carrying its own digits (`PROJ2-card#77`) yielded **two** ids, `2` and `77`, and `2` is a real id belonging to an unrelated card — a token whose id is not trailing now yields nothing (visible in the coverage section) rather than minting one. And `--manifest --card-manifest` together is now rc 2 instead of printing one list and dropping the other in silence — the card#5429 shape, and worse in a query mode whose output is machine-read; the rc assertion for it carries a control that each flag alone still answers, after the first spelling of that assertion was observed to pass for the wrong reason (a fixture with no origin makes the baseline fetch fail at rc 2 as well).

### Fixed

- **card#6232** — **`next-dl --peek` no longer reports an already-allocated DL: it reads the board's non-consuming DL-sequence counter, and falls back to the offline `max+1` scan only when that endpoint is absent.** `--peek` went straight to the offline scan, which takes its maximum over DL numbers that reached a **card** — so a number that was CLAIMED but never STAMPED is invisible to it and the answer under-reports by one per burned claim. Measured on a real board before the fix: `--peek` returned `DL-0220` while the counter's authoritative `next` was **222** (`last_value: 221`, `live_max: 219` — two allocations that never reached a card), i.e. the tool handed back a number the server had already issued. A DL is a **correlation key**, so a reused one silently merges two unrelated work items, and the offline scan cannot ever detect that state: it is a floor, not the truth. Only the counter knows the claimed-but-unstamped numbers. **What makes `--peek` non-consuming is skipping the CLAIM, not skipping the read.** The two DL-sequence routes differ only in method, path and response field, so rather than ship a second copy of the transport they are **one `dl_sequence_call` primitive** with the wrappers passing those three plus their own error wording (canon #5, at the second caller rather than the Nth): the first draft of this fix was ~18 duplicated lines held in agreement by a comment asking the next editor to keep them in sync, and a drift there would have failed no test — it would just have made one route quietly answer a `500` from the offline scan. The consolidation is covered from **both** sides, and the claim path's error arms are now asserted for the first time (they were previously exercised only on the happy path): `404 ⇒ fallback`, `500 ⇒ rc 1`, and the message must carry the **claim's** own label and consequence clause rather than the peek's — watched red under an argument-swap mutation (3 assertions) and under `exit 3 → exit 1` (6 assertions, both routes). While consolidating, a stale contract comment was corrected: it assigned "a 2xx with no positive value" to rc 3, but the code has always exited **1** there, and the contract is now stated once on the primitive instead of restated per wrapper. The shared behaviour: **404 or unreachable ⇒ benign fallback** to the scan (pre-endpoint behaviour, unchanged), **a present-but-errored endpoint ⇒ rc 1 with the cause on stderr**, never a quiet answer from the floor — a plausible wrong number minted into a decision log on a bad token is the worst of the three outcomes, and the pre-change tool did exactly that (it answered `DL-0220` on an HTTP 500). A `2xx` carrying no usable value stays a benign fallback, deliberately matching the claim path rather than the error path, since it is indistinguishable from an older response shape. **Consumer impact:** a board whose kanban lacks the inspect route sees **no change**; a board that has it sees `--peek` start reporting the counter's value, which is the point — if your `--peek` and the inspect call disagree, your `next-dl` predates this fix. The default (claim) path is untouched and is asserted so. The new coverage is a **control pair, not two passes**: both cases run against byte-identical board contents (one card stamped `DL-0219`, so the scan's answer is 220 either way) and vary only whether the endpoint answers — 222 is not derivable from the board contents at all, so a fix that ignored the endpoint reds the first assertion and one that broke the fallback reds the second. Watched red on the pre-change tree: **8 assertions failed**, including `mints nothing → got 'DL-0220'` on the errored endpoint, while the fallback and claim-path controls stayed green on both trees. `docs/DL-COUNTER-RECOVERY.md` is corrected in the same change because the code fix is what makes it true — step 1 now notes that the **old** `--peek` returned `liveMax + 1`, the correct-looking low value, and so **hid** the stranding symptom that step exists to find; and step 5 no longer presents `--peek` as agreeing corroboration, since it now reads the same route — one measurement displayed twice. What that line actually checks is the leg the inspect call cannot: that the operator's own board env, token and `api_base` resolve to the board they just fixed. Verified by executing the runbook step rather than asserting it.

- **card#6062** — **the `changelog-card-entry` gate moves out of `ci.yml` into its own workflow so a PR-TITLE edit re-runs it.** The gate's obligation set is derived from the commit subjects since the last tag **plus the PR title** — the title half is the entire reason the check is pre-merge, since under squash-merge the title *is* the coming subject. But editing a title fires `edited`, and `ci.yml` subscribes GitHub's default `pull_request` set (`opened`/`reopened`/`synchronize`), which does not include it: adding the `card#NNNN` token to a title after the last push left the gate's newest run answering about the **pre-edit** title, so the PR showed green with no entry owed and the omission reddened `dev` on the push after the merge — exactly the state the title read exists to prevent. A job cannot carry its own `types:`, so the fix is a workflow, not a trigger tweak: `.github/workflows/changelog-card-entry.yml` carries the job verbatim (`fetch-depth: 0`, `PR_TITLE` through the environment) under `types: [opened, edited, reopened, synchronize]`. This is the **same class** the release-artifact gate closed above — a gate whose verdict is a function of a PR field that only `edited` reports — with a different field (title, not base); `edited` also fires on body edits, accepted at one checkout plus a `git log` and a grep. The job **keeps its name**, so the `changelog-card-entry` status-check context is unchanged for anything keyed on it, and it keeps `ci.yml`'s `push: [main, dev]` leg, so the post-merge backstop on the integration branch is not traded away for the pre-merge fix. **`ci-matrix-parity-selftest.sh` had to be generalised in the same change or it would have called a genuinely-run test unrun:** its population was `ci.yml` alone, and it is now the **workflow directory filtered by trigger** — a workflow counts only if its `on:` subscribes `pull_request`, so a test named only by the push-only `auto-tag-version`/`release-promote-cards` workflows still reads as unrun rather than the rule loosening to "named somewhere". That predicate is exposed as its own projection and asserted in **both** directions against the real directory (admits `ci.yml`, rejects `release-promote-cards.yml`) plus a twin-fixture pair differing only in the trigger, and it was watched red under three mutations (predicate always-true, always-false, and dropping the `on` → `True` lookup that YAML 1.1 makes necessary — pyyaml resolves a bare `on:` key to the boolean, so reading only `doc.get('on')` admits nothing).

- **card#6054** — **the release-artifact gate's own trigger and the `INSTALL.md` §6c recipe gain `edited`, and two surfaces stop claiming artifact contents are read at both ends.** Retargeting a PR's base fires `edited`, not `synchronize`, and the gate's verdict is a function of the base — the merge base drives both the classification and the asserted diff — so a stale verdict survived a base retarget. Both consumer adoptions (kanban, bridge) added `edited` independently at adoption review; the write-site — the §6c recipe both consumers copied — and this repo's own `release-artifacts-gate.yml` still carried the omission, so the next consumer copying the recipe would re-mint it. Also synced: the gate's fetch-depth comment and the `release-artifacts/action.yml` description said the script reads "the version file plus the artifact contents at both ends"; it reads the version file at both ends and artifact contents at **head only** (`git show`/`cat-file` on head) — now stated exactly, matching the wording both consumers already corrected locally. The §6c recipe's fetch-depth comment ("both ends of the range are read") was vague rather than false — true of the version file — and is tightened in the same pass. The check script and the action's inputs are unchanged.

### Docs

- **card#6169** — **`.github/dependabot.yml` now records why this repository's Dependabot *security* updates are off, as a permanent posture rather than an unexplained gap.** Security updates ignore `target-branch:` and open against the repository's **default** branch, which here carries the version/tag invariant (`VERSION` + `auto-tag-version.yml`) that a dependency PR structurally cannot satisfy: it never bumps `VERSION`, so merging one on the default branch leaves it at a version an older tag already claims and auto-tag correctly refuses to re-mint it, leaving the branch untagged. Vulnerability **alerts remain enabled** — detection is intact, only the automatic security-PR channel is off — and the *version* updates configured in that file continue to honour `target-branch: dev`, so bumps keep arriving on the integration branch on cadence; anything urgent is hand-cut as a PR to `dev` and reaches the release branch through a normal release, never auto-patched onto it. The comment states that the posture is **permanent and does not lift**, because an unexplained disabled channel invites a later reader to re-enable it as tidy-up and re-arm the failure. It also pins the precondition a reader should **re-check rather than trust**: the posture holds only while the alert channel on the default branch is non-vacuous — verified for this repo as no package manifests at all, 4 workflow files, and `github-actions` as the sole declared ecosystem, so the alert channel covers the entire scanned surface here. **Consumer impact: none.** No `bin/` CLI, no `promote`/`release-artifacts` action surface, and no vendored file changes — comments only, with the parsed YAML asserted identical to before and the assertion watched to fail under both a syntax break and a `target-branch` mutation. The reasoning generalises to any repo whose default branch carries a version/tag invariant; the setting itself is per-repository and is inherited by nothing.

## [0.25.0] - 2026-08-07

**Minor — the declared release-artifact set becomes an assertion instead of a checklist, and `board-card-start` gains `-h`/`--help`.** 5 PRs since v0.24.0 (#217–#221). Minor per `VERSIONING.md § Bump sizing` — new user-visible surface (`bin/release-artifacts-check`, the `release-artifacts/` composite action, `board-card-start -h`) alongside the fixes. **⚠ UPGRADING — one narrowing:** `agent-board-toolkit-drift-check` now refuses a third positional at rc 2 where it previously read the first two and reported `drift-check: OK`. Changed vendorable files this release: `bin/agent-board-toolkit-drift-check`, `bin/agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`, `bin/board-card-start`, `bin/release-pr-body`, plus the new `bin/release-artifacts-check` and `release-artifacts/action.yml`; `promote/action.yml` and the board-writing CLIs (`bin/kbcard`, `bin/_kb-board-lib.sh`, `bin/promote-released-cards`) are unchanged.

### Added

- **card#5910** — **`bin/release-artifacts-check` + the `release-artifacts/` composite action: `.release-pr.json`'s `artifacts` array stops being a checklist and becomes an assertion.** That array is the explicit **must-move-together** release set, and its only consumer was `release-pr-body`, which **prints** it as `- [ ]` lines in the generated body. A printer is not an asserter, so the class defect — *a guard that reads a proper subset of a must-move-together file set cannot fail on the member it never reads* — held here with a subset of size **zero**: nothing in this repo checked that `VERSION`, `docs/CHANGELOG.md`'s `[X.Y.Z]` section and `CLAUDE.md § Recent releases`'s row moved together, so a forgotten one shipped in silence. The new tool takes `--base`/`--head` and asserts the PR's **own** changes: the fork point `merge-base(base, head)` is resolved once and drives both the version read and the diff, never base's tip — a base branch that moves after the fork can otherwise spuriously satisfy the moved-leg for a member the PR never touched, or make a real release PR read as "version unchanged" and skip the whole gate. It expands each entry's `{{version}}` and any single-level `{a,b}` brace set, and asserts three things per member: it is an exact line of the captured diff, it still **exists** at head (a deletion appears in a diff, so the moved-leg alone passes one), and it **agrees** with the version — a `## [X.Y.Z]` section line for the `[{{version}}] section` shape, existence alone where the filename carries the version, else the version as a fixed string in the content. **Every** failing member is reported (`::error::` annotations plus an `N of M` summary), not just the first. Release-PR classification is by version **VALUE**, deliberately not "the version file appears in the diff": a repo may point `version_file` at a whole config file (kanban's is `config/app.php`), where the file-moved test misfires on any unrelated edit — base value == head value means "not a release PR", exit 0, so the gate is safe to run on every PR with no `paths:` filter. It **fails closed** (rc 2, naming which end of the range, the path and the `fetch-depth: 0` precondition) when the version file cannot be read at either end or the merge base cannot be resolved at all, rather than reading an unreachable base as "not a release PR" — that misclassification is a silent non-run of the whole gate. Adopted by this repo in [`release-artifacts-gate.yml`](../.github/workflows/release-artifacts-gate.yml) — which makes the check **red**, not unmergeable: it becomes binding through `VERSIONING.md` § Release flow step 8 ("wait for ALL CI checks"), or mechanically once its job is added to `main`'s required status checks. Post-merge `auto-tag-version.yml` stays **fail-soft by design**, because at that point the fix is a retag rather than a commit. `tests/release-artifacts-selftest.sh` builds a throwaway fixture repo under `mktemp` and watches each defect class red on its own branch (member not moved · CHANGELOG moved without its section · member deleted · unreadable version file at the fork point · a base branch that moved after the fork, in both the spurious-moved and version-masking directions · an unresolvable merge base · two failures reported as two), with the version-unchanged and fully-correct branches as the controls that keep "fails on X" from also being true of a tool that fails on everything.

- **card#5354** — **`board-card-start` gains `-h`/`--help`, printing its usage line to stdout at rc 0.** Before this, a hand-typed `board-card-start --help` fell through to the `-*` unknown-option arm, whose remedy line — *"for a branch name that starts with `-`, pass it after `--`"* — reads as advice to run `board-card-start -- --help`, which would target a **branch literally named `--help`**. That refusal text was also the only usage surface a hand-typed invocation ever reached. The arm sits **inside** the end-of-options guard, so `--` keeps claiming its argument: `board-card-start -- --help` still means the branch `--help`, not a help request (a selftest asserts exactly this, and goes red if the arm is hoisted above the guard). The refusal text is deliberately **unchanged** — it is phrased conditionally (*"for a branch name that starts with `-`"*), so it never instructed anyone to run that command; the actionable defect was the **absent** help surface, not the wording. Registration in `help-output-selftest.sh` was **machine-forced, not remembered**: adding the arm put the literal `--help` into the file, which immediately reddened the card#5339 completeness leg (`unaccounted bins with --help … got 'board-card-start'`) until the bin was named in `EXCLUDED` — the one-liner form, which cannot satisfy a contiguous-header line-count equality. That leg's registry arithmetic is now phrased against the arrays rather than restating their lengths, so it cannot go stale as the registry grows.

### Fixed

- **card#5429** — **`agent-board-toolkit-drift-check` no longer discards a third positional in silence, and it now names which argument is empty.** ⚠ **UPGRADING — a narrowing:** `agent-board-toolkit-drift-check <toolkit> <repo> <anything-else>` previously exited **0** with `drift-check: OK`, reading the first two arguments and dropping the rest without a word; it now exits **2** with `unexpected extra argument: <arg>`. This is the failure mode a gate must never have — the tool *is* the drift gate, so a caller who believed they passed a scoping or strictness flag got a green drift report about something other than what they asked for. The other half of the same rule was implemented but **anonymously**: an empty positional was refused at rc 2 behind a bare usage line that never said *which* of the two slots was empty, where the three bins card#5343 consolidated all name it (`<repo-dir> is empty (an unexpanded variable?)`) — with two positionals the naming is worth more, not less. Both halves now match the shared wording. **Found by the canon-#7 sibling audit run for card#5343's consolidation PR, not by report:** that consolidation covered the three sites the card named, and `bin/install-board-hooks`' own arg-loop comment had already written down this exact rationale ("Silently ignoring an extra positional is the same hazard one step further out") without it ever being applied to the sibling. **Deliberately not a fourth adoption of `kb_require_positional`,** on the ground the repo already applies to `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`: this tool is a **detector of `_kb-board-lib.sh`** — the content-drift loop diffs it, the MISSING-LIB leg reads it — so sourcing the lib would make the detector depend on the artifact under test, and a drifted or broken lib would take out the tool that exists to report it. The primitive is also single-slot by construction, and generalising it to N slots would cost the property that makes it simple in order to serve the repo's only two-positional bin — extraction belongs at the second real caller and there is no second. With fixed arity and no argument loop, `"$#"` answers both halves directly; the reasoning is recorded in `docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md` Stage C and the shared diagnostic wording is pinned by a header comment. **No caller is affected:** every invocation of the tool anywhere — the `drift-check-selftest` CI job (`. .`), the selftest, and the three documented examples in `INSTALL.md` §6b / `UPGRADE.md` §3 and §4 — passes exactly two positionals, and no consumer repo invokes it at all since both vendored drift-gates were retired for the SHA-pinned composite action. `tests/drift-check-fixture-selftest.sh` gains coverage of all nine input classes (arity 0/1/2/3+ crossed with which slot is empty), asserting on the **message** rather than the rc, because every one of these shapes already exited 2 — an rc-only assertion passes just as well when the refusal comes from the wrong guard, which is exactly how the anonymous case survived a green suite. Each new assertion was **observed red** under a mutation that removes the guard it covers, including the pre-card guard restored verbatim (which reds the three naming assertions and nothing else) and the empty-before-extra ordering that no other case can witness; the mutation harness positive-controls itself on both axes — the edit must actually change the file, and the suite must still run the full baseline assertion count, which caught one mutation whose pattern never applied. A repo-wide sibling audit over every `bin/` and `hooks/` file found **no other unguarded positional site** (n=1, no consolidation owed): the rest either adopt the primitive, carry `install-board-hooks`' standalone mirror, or refuse every unrecognised token through a `*)` arm — the audit method positive-controlled against the pre-fix bin, which it flags. `promote/action.yml` vendor surface unchanged.
- **card#5874** — **`release-pr-body` no longer reports a correct body as a failed generation when the commit range contains no ref-token match.** Its final statement was `[ -n "$MANIFEST" ] && printf '<!-- release-manifest… -->'`; with an empty manifest the test fails, and because it was the **last command the script ran**, its status became the script's exit status — a fully-written, correct body returned at **rc 1**. `set -e` cannot catch this: the left arm of an `&&` list is exempt, which is why the script ran to completion first. It contradicted the tool's own stated contract in two places (the header's "an empty range yields an empty (but valid) bundled section, not a failure", and `emit_manifest`'s "a release with no ref tokens is valid, not a failure" — that function already guarded itself with `|| true`; the final statement was the one place the intent was not carried through). Found while cutting the v0.24.0 release PR, and it had become **unconditional** for this repo rather than occasional: commit subjects migrated to `card#NNNN` while `.release-pr.json` still sets `ref_token_regex` to `DL-[0-9]+`, so the manifest is now always empty here and every future release cut would have tripped it. The optional-footer branch is now an `if` block, and the script closes on an explicit `exit 0` so the tail cannot leak a status at all — the narrower fix would have left the next edit free to re-introduce it. Verified on the real repo, not only the fixture: same command and same range exit **1** before and **0** after, with a byte-identical body. The selftest gained the no-token-range case it never had — **watched RED on the pre-fix tree**, with the same range under a *matching* regex asserted as the discriminating control, so a vacuous pass is not available to it. A repo-wide sibling audit over all 48 tracked shell files (both sites: script tail and function tail, the scan positive-controlled against a known-leaky fixture) found **no other instance** — every other `&&`-tail is a deliberate predicate function, an assertion helper that ends in `&& ok || bad`, or a fail-soft `|| true`, and the two scripts whose last textual line is a bare `fi` were read in full (they close on `return "$rc"` and `exit 0` respectively). The stale `ref_token_regex` itself is deliberately **not** changed here: switching it interacts with `promote-released-cards`, which matches the token's numeric part against `payload.dl_number`, so a `card#` spelling would compare card ids to DL numbers — tracked separately.
- **card#5910** — **the `promote-action-selftest` flag-parity check now DERIVES the wrapper's flag list instead of enumerating three literals.** It was three hand-written `grep -q -- '--dls)'` lines, i.e. the same subset-of-a-must-move-together-set shape as the entry above: a **fourth** action input would have been guarded by nothing, and no assertion can fail on the flag it never reads. The adjacent python step that already parses `promote/action.yml` now extracts every `--flag`-shaped token appearing **anywhere on an `args+=( … )` append line**, plus any on the exec line, prints the derived list, and asserts each has a `--flag)` case arm in `bin/promote-released-cards`. The scan is per line, so a multi-line append would derive nothing from its continuation lines (today's wrapper writes one append per line). Scanning the whole line rather than anchoring on the append's first token is what closes the two spellings a first-token pattern let through in silence — `args+=("--quoted-flag" …)` and `args+=(--a --b)`; it deliberately **over**-collects (a `--`-leading value literal would also demand an arm), which errs red rather than green. The derivation carries its own control (a non-empty list containing `--dry-run`), so a parser that silently derives zero flags **fails** rather than passing vacuously. Four controls were watched to fire: a quoted flag, a second flag in one append, an exec-line flag, and an emptied run block. **The exec-line half collects nothing from today's wrapper** (which passes only `"${args[@]}"`) — it guards a future direct-flag spelling and is verified by that mutation, not by the live file. A sibling `release-artifacts-action-selftest` job gives the new composite action the same wiring guard, deriving its assertion set from the action's own declared `inputs` so a fourth input cannot escape the loop.

- **card#5876** — **every surface that told a reader to `git checkout <tag>` now says which topology that is correct for, because on the recommended one it is not.** `docs/UPGRADE.md` §5 offered `git -C ~/agent-board-toolkit checkout <previous-tag>` as the host rollback and asserted on the same line that "rollback is always safe" — true about *state* (the toolkit stores none) and false about *topology*: on the INSTALL §1 **Option A** clone, whose whole contract is that "upgrades are then just `git pull`", it **detaches HEAD**, and §2's own `git pull --ff-only` then exits **1** (`You are not currently on a branch.`) — measured on a throwaway clone against an attached-HEAD control that exits 0. So the doc walked the reader into precisely the pinned topology INSTALL §2's warning box exists to keep them out of, where the silent cost is that the next release simply never arrives. Fixed on all four surfaces the sibling audit found, not just the reported one: §5 now states that rollback IS a deliberate temporary pin and gives the return path (and names `reset --hard` as the wrong escape — it diverges the branch and breaks `--ff-only` too); §2 names the detached case at the point of use; `VERSIONING.md` step 11 — where the same command is **correct**, its target being a deliberately-detached second checkout — now carries that scope on the line itself, since a line whose correctness lives only in a `<pinned-runtime>` placeholder is one copy-paste from being wrong somewhere else. **And `agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`'s own remediation, the highest-traffic instance** (it fires at every SessionStart on a stale host, and it fired on the reporting seat), **now branches on topology** rather than assuming a pin: a checkout on a branch is told to `pull --ff-only` and why a tag checkout would detach it; only an already-detached one is told to advance the pin. Reported by a downstream adopter against a v0.24.0 announcement snippet that had relayed the maintainer deploy line to a clone install; the adopter ran the pull instead and verified the identical commit landed. The selftest gained the clone-tracking-a-branch topology it never had — watched RED on the pre-fix tree, with the existing detached-pin case asserted as the discriminating control so the two arms cannot collapse into one.

## [0.24.0] - 2026-08-04

**Minor — `patch` can finally correct the five fields that were settable only at birth, swimlanes gain their write and laneless-read sides, and the release's largest theme is that a dozen surfaces which nothing asserted on now have gates that were watched to fail.** 37 PRs since v0.23.1 (#178–#214). Minor per `VERSIONING.md § Bump sizing` — new user-visible surface (`patch --name/--tags/--type/--external-id/--origin`, `create-card --swimlane`, `list --swimlane none`, a new `board-session-close` leg) alongside the fixes.

**⚠ UPGRADING — four things narrow what the tools accept.** Each was a case where a wrong input was answered with a default path at rc 0. (1) `kbcard "" <verb>` printed usage and exited **0**; an empty first argument is now a failed expansion, not a help request, and exits non-zero — a caller that checked `rc` was reading a no-op as a completed write (card#5276). (2) An explicitly-empty **positional** argument is refused across the three bins that took one, where it was previously invisible and let the next argument silently take its place — `install-board-hooks "" <repo>` installed into `<repo>` (card#5343, card#5200). (3) `board-card-start` now refuses an empty/unknown/extra argument and honours `--lint` in any position; it previously dropped the flag and moved a card for real (card#5333). (4) Guards spelled as bash bracket **ranges** are pinned to ASCII, so under a UTF-8 shell they no longer match the wider set the locale's collation admitted (card#5409). One further observable: `patch --triaged` no longer re-**sorts** the card's tags (card#5776). `promote/action.yml`'s vendor surface is unchanged.

### Added
- **card#5776** — **five card fields were settable only at birth: `kbcard create-card` wrote `name`, `tags`, `card_type_id`, `external_id` and `payload.origin`, and `patch` could never change any of them.** A card minted wrong stayed wrong. The shipped consequence was a **board record asserting something the code never did**: a consumer's Dependabot cards take their name once from the PR title, Dependabot then **retargets the PR in place**, and nothing re-reads the title at promotion — so a card sat in Shipped-to-dev claiming TypeScript 7.0.2 shipped while the merged diff landed 6.0.3, directly contradicting a sibling card saying TS 7 was blocked, with the *wrong* one reading as resolved. With no `--name` neither title could be corrected, and the correction lived in each description behind a stale-title header. `patch` now takes **`--name` / `--tags` / `--type` / `--external-id` / `--origin`**, each writing the field its identically-named `create-card` flag writes at birth. Additive: an omitted flag leaves its field alone. **The API side was PROBED before any of this was built, not assumed** — the card gated implementation on it, and a response echoing `name` proves only that it is part of the representation (`GET` echoes it too), so each field was asserted by **GET re-read** with `board_id` (422 "prohibited" on update) as the negative control that made the probe able to fail. Two behaviours are worth knowing: **`--tags` REPLACES the whole list** (the API stores `tags` as one list and replaces it wholesale — pass the full intended set to add one; `--triaged` still appends and composes with it), and **`--type` drops any `type:*` tag the card carried** before writing the board's native `card_type_id` or the `type:<alias>` fallback tag. That strip is load-bearing on a **mixed** board, where `list --type <alias>` resolves an alias with no native id through the tag clause: a card retyped to a native type while keeping its old `type:` tag would answer as **two types at once** — the same quiet-wrong the retype exists to fix. Everything resolvable offline (alias spelling, `--external-id` numeric-ness, `--column`, `--swimlane`) is resolved **before any request**, so a typo fails rc 2 having issued none — asserted against a request log with a positive control, not assumed.
- **card#5385** — **`patch` had `--description` but no `--name`, so there was no supported way to retitle a card.** Discharged as the `--name` member of card#5776 above rather than separately: the two would have been the same parser arm, and shipping it alone was what the class card existed to prevent. The consumer discipline the class records is the reason it was a toolkit FR at all — the reporting agent did **not** hand-roll a raw API `PATCH` to fix the names, because that forks a second write path around the wrapper that owns board writes.
- **card#5767** — **a shipped card's `[Unreleased]` entry was a convention enforced by nothing, and 3 of the 24 cards merged since v0.23.1 had no entry at all.** The rule was not merely ungated, it was **unwritten**: `VERSIONING.md` documented only the release-time CHANGELOG update (step 4), so the per-PR obligation existed solely as 21-out-of-24 precedent. The third miss is why the new gate keys on a line-initial bullet rather than a mention: card#5374's only appearance in this file was a **stale forward reference** inside card#5359's entry — *"card#5374 proposes the selftest that would have caught both directions"*, written before #191 shipped it — so a release cut from that file told a reader the gate was proposed, not delivered. Same shape this repo closed three times before by building the tripwire instead of re-fixing instances (card#5355, card#5389, card#5374 itself): a list a human keeps in sync, with no check. **The gate** ([`tests/changelog-card-entry-selftest.sh`](../tests/changelog-card-entry-selftest.sh), its own CI job) derives BOTH sides and hand-lists neither — obligations from commit subjects since the last release tag **plus the PR title** (under squash-merge the title *is* the coming subject, which makes the check pre-merge instead of reddening `dev` after the omission has landed), discharges from the file, scoped to the sections above that tag's own section so an entry under `[0.20.0]` cannot discharge a commit that landed after v0.23.1. A commit with no `card#` token owes nothing, so the exemption for `chore(deps)`/`docs(orientation)` syncs is the absence of a trigger rather than a second registry to maintain. The job takes `fetch-depth: 0`, and the three checkout preconditions are **hard exits before any assertion runs**, because the live leg asserts an absence and each of them can make it answer "" for a reason unrelated to the repo being clean. The load-bearing one is a **missing `## [<last tag>]` stop header** — it widens the scan to the whole file, which errs GREEN silently. The shallow-clone refusal was measured rather than assumed and is a **diagnostic, not a second correctness catch**: a shallow clone yields no wrong answer (either no tag is reachable and `describe` fails, or it is reachable and the range is complete), but a real `--depth 1` clone carrying all 36 tags still resolves none — so without the shallow arm that checkout is told to fetch tags it already has. **Instances fixed in the same change:** card#5371 and card#5374 below, and card#5359's stale forward reference. Weakest property: a green run means no shipped card is undocumented — it says nothing about whether a bullet's prose is accurate.
- **card#5766** — **`kbcard list --swimlane none` (or `0`) filters to the LANELESS cards.** The write side could create a state the read side could not query: `create-card --swimlane none` (card#5671) and `patch --swimlane none` (card#4714, v0.19.0) both deliberately birth or leave a card lane-free, while `list --swimlane none` resolved `none` as a lane *name*, found no `KB_SWIMLANE_*` mapping, and failed rc 2 "swimlane 'none' is not defined on this board". That asymmetry was left open on purpose in #209 — widening what `list` accepts is a different decision from adding a create flag — and routed to the operator who actually runs lanes. **The answer came back as a census, not a preference** (roundtable #205, sola-pm, board 3 / 721 cards): laneless is not a transient state there but a standing **7%** — 50 cards, of which 23 are legitimately lane-free, 1 is genuinely cross-cutting, and **26 carry a `sola-<repo>:` title prefix naming exactly one repo**. The competing "laneless because untriaged" hypothesis was tested and does not survive (two spot-checked members are both tagged `triaged` and sit in `shipped_to_dev`), and a control rules out the prefix being a laneless dialect (the same prefix appears on 25 of 671 *laned* cards — the 52%-vs-3.7% concentration is the anomaly, not the convention). The decisive point is not that those 26 are misfiled — that is *unestablished, and unestablishable*, because no query returns them; they surfaced only as a side effect of answering the question. **The trap this deliberately avoids:** `_kbc_swimlane_value()` maps `none` → JSON `null`, and the list filter treats JSON `null` as *filter absent* — so the naive reuse of the write-side rule returns the **entire board** while looking correct. The filter is therefore transported as a **string** (`--arg`, absent = `""`) rather than JSON (`--argjson`, absent = `null`), which keeps "filter absent" distinguishable from "filter on laneless" and makes the laneless value the projection's own existing key for a lane-free card, `(null | tostring)` == `"null"` — the same key derivation the name-map lookup already uses, not a new comparison. The `none`/`0` spelling is hoisted into one owner, `_kbc_swimlane_is_laneless_ref()`, now shared by all three call sites (canon #5 — extracted at the third real caller, not copied a third time); `0` counts because lane ids are positive, so it never named a real lane, and it must be recognised *before* `swimlane_id()`, which passes any numeric through unvalidated. **Unchanged, and now extended to `list`:** a lane literally *named* `none` is unreachable by name and must be addressed by id; a typo'd lane name still fails rc 2 and enumerates the board's defined lanes. **Validated on a real swimlaned board** (a sandbox board carrying an actual lane, not a synthetic fixture): with one laned and one lane-free probe card present the populations genuinely differ — whole board 9, `--swimlane none` 8, `--swimlane 0` 8, `--swimlane <name>` exactly the laned probe — so the whole-board regression was *observable* there rather than hidden behind a board where every card happens to be laneless; probes hard-deleted, zero residue. All 13 new assertions were driven RED by five targeted mutations (JSON transport restored → the filter returns all 3 of 3 synthetic cards; laneless branch removed → `none` rejected rc 2; the `0` arm dropped; the predicate forced true; the laneless key derivation diverged), each reverted with the binary confirmed byte-identical after.
- **card#5671** — **`kbcard create-card --swimlane <name|id>` births a card directly on a lane** (roundtable #205, sola-pm). `create-card` accepted 16 flags and `--swimlane` was not among them, so it fell to the unknown-arg arm with rc 2 and no write — while `patch` and `list` both advertised `[--swimlane NAME-OR-ID]` two lines away in the *same* usage block, which is what made it reachable by reflex. Minting onto a lane therefore required create-then-patch, leaving a laneless window any lane-keyed reader or automation can observe. The create `POST` honours `swimlane_id` at birth — measured on a swimlaned board and asserted by a GET re-read rather than by the 2xx — so the flag rides the create body directly, with no internal create+patch composition and no laneless window. `--swimlane none` (or `0`) births a card explicitly laneless; an unresolved name fails rc 2 **before any write**, and that strictness deliberately did not change (the defect was the asymmetry that made the flag reachable, not the check that caught it). Shipped in #209; this entry was omitted from that PR and is added here — the gate that would have caught the omission is filed as card#5767.
- **card#5740** (consolidation program, test-harness axis — the class closure card#5740's own fix left open) — **deleting the ten copies of `has()` did not stop an eleventh: the "lives here and nowhere else" contract was a prelude comment, enforced by nothing.** That is precisely the shape Stage B was built to remove (`ci-matrix-parity-selftest`'s `# KEEP THIS LIST IN SYNC`), re-minted one layer down — fixing N copies without the guard that forbids the N+1th leaves the cause in place, so the class was reported closed while its mechanism was intact. `tests/prelude-shadow-selftest.sh` is that guard: it fails when any `tests/*-selftest.sh` re-declares a helper `_selftest-prelude.sh` defines. The helper set is **derived from the prelude**, never restated — a hardcoded list would be this program's own defect a third time — and the deliberate variants (`kb-board-lib-selftest`'s `expect_rc`/`expect_out`, which route failures through the shared `eq`) are allow-listed **by name**, so adding a shadow costs an explicit edit to the guard, which is the review moment the ten silent copies never got. A stale allow-list entry — one naming a shadow that no longer exists, which would silently re-permit a future copy of that name — fails too. **All three bash spellings are recognised** (`has()`, `has ()`, `function has {`): a name-only regex was written first and caught only the first, which would have let the next copy evade the guard by one keystroke while reading as covered. **Test-only — no `bin/` or `hooks/` change, so no vendored surface moves and `promote/action.yml` is untouched.** Every leg was driven red before being trusted: re-adding a local `has()` in each of the three spellings, renaming a sanctioned shadow, and removing `has` from the prelude each took exactly the intended leg red, all mutations reverted and the tree re-verified. Suite 26/26, `shellcheck -S error` clean. Also corrects the prelude's own comment, which said **eleven** selftests had defined `has()` locally — measured at the pre-fix commit, it was eleven *files* including the prelude itself, i.e. **ten** selftests, matching the card and this changelog; the miscount existed nowhere else in the repo.
- **card#5444** — **the consolidation program shaped a dozen changes across this release and left no record in the repo of *why*.** Its reasoning lived only in an agent-local plan file, so the repo carried the outcomes as CHANGELOG entries with nothing stating the invariants a *further* consolidation must not violate — and the single most load-bearing finding (that "empty vs absent" is **three** invariants requiring **opposite** answers on the same input shape, which is why one value-guard cannot own all three) was recoverable only by re-deriving it. Two plan revisions had already got that wrong. **New [`docs/CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md`](CONSOLIDATION-PLAN.md)**, linked from `README.md`'s Get-started list as the thing to read before changing anything shared across these tools. It records the diagnosis, the ground rules (including *mechanism belongs in the primitive, policy at the caller*, and *add, never replace*), what each stage actually shipped, what was **dropped and why** — the `kb_parse` sibling parser, the comment sweep, and the `promote-released-cards` lib adoption, whose four preconditions are the strongest argument in the program *against* a consolidation — and the claims review disproved, kept rather than quietly deleted. **It is a reconciliation, not a copy of the source plan**, which would have been false on arrival in five ways: the source contradicts itself on the positional axis (its own context section proves `kb_require_value` cannot absorb it, while its Stage C says to extend it there — card#5343 settled it with a *separate* `kb_require_positional`); it describes the dropped lib-adoption stage as live and this document as pending; it prescribes deriving `help-output-selftest`'s `CLIS` from the header idiom, which card#5339 measured as **worse than the status quo** (a required-side derivation fails **open**); its supporting claim that the two host guards are *"identical — verified over 21 hostile URLs × 4 expected-hosts"* does not match `tests/kb-host-guard-selftest.sh`, which compares them over **20 URLs at one expected host** plus the unset/empty fail-closed regimes (identity supported, numbers not); and every one of its `file:line` citations had gone stale across sixteen merged PRs. The new document therefore **cites by symbol, never by line**, and says so as a rule — verified while writing it, when its own draft cited a measured negative result at the call site card#5343 had already moved it away from. Docs only; no tool, test, or vendored surface changes.
- **card#5371** — **`board-session-close`'s "Archive-eligible done cards" leg delegates to the `_kbc-archive-eligible.py` sibling, and nothing asserted the delegation.** `grep -nE 'Archive-eligible|ae_helper|_kbc-archive-eligible'` over the selftest returned zero, with a positive control on the same file returning 3. The leg's rc contract **inverts** the inverse-drift leg's — a missing sibling and a non-zero helper each warn on stderr and leave the ritual's exit code alone, where a failing reconcile hook propagates — and both answers are defensible, which is exactly why the difference needed pinning: nothing stopped the two legs converging. The helper is resolved as a sibling of the bin with no env override, so the cases run a COPY of the bin with a FAKE sibling beside it, making the delegate's rc/stdout/stderr an input rather than an observation. Every "the ritual's rc is UNCHANGED" claim is paired with a presence witness on the same fixture, so the rc channel is known live before any absence claim is made.
- **card#5389** — **`agent-board-toolkit-runtime-check`'s `TOOLS` array was a second hand-maintained enumeration of `bin/`, it under-covered by 3 of 13, and its comment claimed it was derived.** The comment read *"every `bin/` entry of the runtime this script itself belongs to, falling back to the known names when run standalone"*; there was no derivation anywhere in the file (whole file read, not grepped) — the array was always the only tool set, and it was missing `dl-a0-backfill-triaged`, `dl-a1-register-field` and **the check itself**. Both failure legs iterate `TOOLS`, so the three were structurally invisible to the missing-from-PATH warning — whose own comment names the failure it exists to catch as *"the symlink loop predates the tool's release, the v0.12.0 `adopt-to-dl` class"*, which is precisely what `dl-a1-register-field` is — and to the stale-COPY byte-compare. The tool that exists to prove an install is honest could not see 3 of the 13 tools it installs, and said nothing about the gap. **Fixed by completing the list and GATING it, not by deriving it** (the card's (a)-vs-(b) fork, decided on measurement): a derivation is not available where it is needed — the script is standalone by design and runs from `PATH`, where under the copies topology the directory beside it is a general-purpose `~/.local/bin`, not a toolkit `bin/` — and (a) keeps a static fallback anyway, i.e. it is (b) **plus** a directory-sniffing heuristic, with the fallback still free to rot. A static list also behaves BETTER when this script and the runtime it judges are different versions: the ships-it-but-`PATH`-lacks-it warning re-checks `$root/bin/$m` per root, so a superset probe never cries wolf about a tool the judged runtime does not ship, whereas a derivation would have to pick one root's answer. Since the list ships in the same release as the `bin/` it enumerates, asserting set equality in CI gives every released runtime the same answer a derivation would. **The check itself is now in the set** — a stale copy of the checker is the one most worth reporting — resolved through `command -v` like every other tool, so running it by absolute path from a checkout that is not on `PATH` cannot make it report itself as a second runtime. **The gate READS THE SET BACK FROM THE TOOL rather than parsing its source:** on a `PATH` carrying none of the tools every name lands in `missing` and the no-roots arm prints the whole array on stdout, so the assertion is about the set the code ACTUALLY probes and survives any refactor of how the array is spelled — a source grep would instead pin the spelling and could pass over an array the code no longer reads. Driven **red** under three mutations against the live tree: a real bin dropped from `TOOLS` (named by the first direction), a phantom added (named by the second), and the readback channel broken by forcing `--quiet`, which fires both positive controls — the emptiness both absence assertions would otherwise pass on. **What a green run proves — the weakest property:** that two NAME SETS agree.
